These are the only accepted mathematical inputs.\n\n## 1. Exact scope and local notation\n\nFor a nonnegative physical word h of length n, the project objective is\nW_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), with the original GD\nupdates x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Here the supremum allows every\nfinite dimension and legal convex L-smooth function, and the attained\nexamples below have the TRUE closest initial radius one and sharp L=1.\nThe original target concerns inf_h W_n(h) relative to the exact all-split\nU_n^ZJ; a lower witness on selected words cannot settle that infimum.\n\nThe candidate's local parameters are M=M_mc>=2, B=B_mc>=2,\nA=A_mc>=4B+4, and epsilon in [0,1]. Define\n\n    nu=sqrt(2A-4),\n    p=e_1/M,\n    q=(-2e_1+nu e_2)/M,\n    Q=||q||^2=2A/M^2,\n    r=-q/2+sqrt((B/2-1/4)Q)e_3,\n    R=||r||^2=BQ/2,       <q,r>=-Q/2.\n\nThe independent q and r coordinates will be useful; their first\ncoordinates are q_1=-2/M and r_1=1/M. The audited words are exactly\n\n    h_three=(L_0,AM+1,L_1,BM+1,L_2,1+M epsilon),\n    h_return=(L_0,AM+1,L_1,BM+1,L_2,CM+1,L_3,1+M epsilon).\n\nNo arbitrary word is asserted to admit either form. Each resulting\ncompleted function may depend on the full known word, but is fixed\nbefore the actual run and is the same function at every query.\n\nThe three analytical attack groups were: (i) actual chronology and\nevery remote anchor, including parameter boundaries and the old first-r\nquery; (ii) every reference cut, complex-lens scope, benchmark direction\nand padded horizon; (iii) true-radius and collapsed-policy scope and\nthe inherited-hierarchy diagnostic. No decisive counterexample to the\nfinal stated conclusions was found. This absence alone is not the\nreason for the verdict: the critical claims are rederived below.\n\n## 2. Static old function and every reached query\n\nLet D=conv{0,p,q,r} and\n\n    F_old(x)=max_(g in D){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.\n\nThe maximum is attained uniquely because D is compact and the objective\nis strictly concave; the maximizer is P_D(x). The projection normal\ncondition says, for u=x-g,\n\n    <u,b-g><=0 for every b in D.\n\nIt also says g maximizes the support J(u)=max(0,<p,u>,<q,u>,<r,u>).\nThus x=u+g, delta=J(u)=<g,u>, and\nF_old(x)=delta+||g||^2/2. Conversely, any displayed pair with g in D\nand this support condition is the UNIQUE projection pair. Tied support\nplanes do not make the envelope gradient ambiguous.\n\nFor any two old pairs, direct cancellation in the signed original\nresidual of fact1252 gives\n\n    Q_xy=F_old(x)-F_old(y)+<g_x,y-x>+||g_x-g_y||^2/2\n        =<u_y,g_x-g_y><=0.\n\nThis works for every ordered pair, remote pairs, duplicate positions,\nand the zero optimum. It uses the actual support condition, not a\nconsecutive-pair surrogate. Projection firm nonexpansiveness supplies\nglobal smoothness one; differentiability and convexity also follow\nfrom this attained conjugate maximum. The slopes are independent,\nso D has interior, where P_D(x)=x and F_old has Hessian I. Its least\nglobal smoothness is therefore exactly one.\n\nBecause 0 is a vertex of D, F_old>=0 and its complete minimum set is\n\n    {x:<p,x><=0, <q,x><=0, <r,x><=0}.\n\nIndeed this condition makes every objective term nonpositive. If any\nof the three inner products is positive, a sufficiently small positive\nmultiple of that vertex belongs to D and makes the objective positive.\nThe p inequality puts all minima in x_1<=0; origin is the unique\nclosest one to e_1 and the true distance is one.\n\nThe following table derives the actual static chronology. Its variable\nt=1+s, where s is the ordinary mass already spent in the current list;\ntherefore 1<=t<=M. The right endpoint t=M is also the selected following\nbridge query. Nonnegativity of every entry ensures every ordinary\npartial mass lies in this interval.\n\n| Stage | Proposed proximal point u | Current support height | Exhaustive other-plane comparisons |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| p | (1-t/M)e_1 | (1-t/M)/M | q height is minus twice this height; r height equals this height; zero is below it |\n| q | X-tq, X=-Ae_1 | (M-t)Q | p height=(-A+2t/M)/M<0; r height=-(M-t)Q/2<=0; zero is below q |\n| r | X_R-tr, X_R=y-BMq | (M-t)R | q height=-BMQ+tQ/2<0; p height=(2-A+2B-t/M)/M<0; zero is below r |\n| returned q | X_D-tq, X_D=z-CMr | (M-t)Q | r height=-CMR+tQ/2<0; p height=(-A+2t/M)/M<0; zero is below q |\n\nFor the first row, the p/r tie holds at EVERY p-stage query. Thus BOTH old terminal values are zero.\nOnly the unqueried terminal completion creates the stated new payoff.\n\n## 3. Complete conjugate-anchor lists and all prefix rows\n\nFor a prefix jet x=u+g, f=delta+||g||^2/2, its conjugate lower\nquadratic from fact1252 simplifies, with no dropped constant, to\n\n    H_jet(v)=||v||^2/2+<u,v>-delta.\n\nWithin one constant-gradient stage both u and delta are affine in\nthe ordinary partial mass. This proves exact equality of the maximum over every\nphysical prefix jet with the endpoint maximum, rather than replacing\nthe interpolation problem by an endpoint relaxation.\n\nHere are all six/eight affine terms, including the original optimum:\n\n| Anchor u | Offset delta | Laws retaining it |\n|---|---|---|\n| 0 | 0 | both; p-stage bridge and optimum give the same affine term |\n| t_P=a e_1 | a/M | both; initial query |\n| y | 0 | both; q-stage ending bridge query |\n| t_Q=y+(M-1)q | (M-1)Q | both; FIRST q query |\n| z | 0 | both; r-stage ending bridge query |\n| t_R=z+(M-1)r | (M-1)R | both; FIRST r query |\n| w | 0 | return; returned-q ending query |\n| t_D=w+(M-1)q | (M-1)Q | return; FIRST returned-q query |\n\nThe distinct affine-anchor counts are six and eight. In particular the\nold FIRST r query is retained after returning to q; its lower quadratic\ndoes not become redundant merely because the endpoint z is retained.\n\nDefine H_three as ||v||^2/2 plus the maximum of the first six affine\nterms, H_return using all eight, and F_three=H_three^*,\nF_return=H_return^*. All ordered old prefix inequalities and the\noptimum jet were proved in Section 2. The maximal-prefix construction\nof accepted fact1252 therefore applies exactly, preserving EVERY old\nprefix position, gradient and value, including repeated queries. Its\nproof gives finite convex C1 globally 1-smooth conjugates. Fix these\nfunctions before running their words. Induction on the physical indices\nthen gives the same prefix updates; no online function change is used.\n\nThe new terminal gradients are not assumed equal to the old gradients.\nAt the terminal, x=v+conv(active anchor slopes) is the precise\nsubgradient optimality condition. It is checked separately below.\nSince each completed H is 1-strongly convex, that condition determines\nthe unique terminal gradient. Incoming AND outgoing terminal rows hold\nfor the resulting actual function; fact1252 is not being used to\ndelete a queried-prefix comparison.\n\n## 4. Three-stage terminal, including every boundary\n\nLet L=B+a, E=t_Q-z=M L q+Mr, and\n\n    D_3=||E||^2=2A(L^2-L+B/2),\n    v_0=-M epsilon r,\n    b_3=<E,v_0>-(M-1)Q=Aa(epsilon-2/M),\n    theta_3=(b_3)_+/D_3,    v_3=v_0-theta_3 E.\n\nFor a in [1/2,1),\nL^2-L+B/2=B^2+B(2a-1/2)+a^2-a>=B^2+B/2-1/4>=B^2.\nThus D_3>=2AB^2 and 0<=theta_3<=epsilon/(2B^2)<=1/8.\nThis pays the convex-combination range and every denominator sign.\n\nAll six affine comparisons can be written explicitly:\n\n* z has value -theta_3<z,E>, with\n  <z,E>=2A(1/2-B)L<0. This value is nonnegative, and is\n  positive when theta_3>0.\n* t_Q minus z has affine value b_3-theta_3 D_3, which is zero\n  when b_3>=0 and nonpositive otherwise.\n* y and 0 have value zero because y is orthogonal to q and r.\n* t_R minus z has value (M-1)(<r,v_3>-R). Here\n  <r,v_3>=(MQ/2)(-B epsilon+a theta_3)<0 for epsilon>0.\n  At epsilon=0 this difference is instead -(M-1)R<0.\n* t_P minus z has value <t_P-z,v_3>-a/M. Directly,\n  <t_P-z,v_0>=-a epsilon and\n  <t_P-z,E>=2L[A(B-1/2)-a]+a>0, so this difference is negative.\n\nIf epsilon<2/M, theta_3=0 and z is active, along with y and 0;\nif epsilon=2/M, t_Q also ties but z alone may still be selected.\nIf epsilon>2/M, z and t_Q are active and theta_3 lies strictly\nbetween zero and one. In every case,\n\n    x_n=z+v_0=v_3+z+theta_3 E in partial H_three(v_3).\n\nAt epsilon=0 the terminal gradient and value are zero and z is a\nminimum. This covers M=2, epsilon=1 as well, where epsilon=2/M;\nthere is no hidden positive-theta assumption at that endpoint.\n\nThe active z term gives the exact conjugate value by completing a\nsquare:\n\n    V_three=2F_three(x_n)\n           =||v_0||^2-(b_3)_+^2/D_3\n           =AB epsilon^2-(Aa(epsilon-2/M)_+)^2/D_3.\n\nSince the loss is at most A epsilon^2/(2B^2),\n\n    AB epsilon^2(1-1/(2B^3))<=V_three<=AB epsilon^2.\n\nThe formula is continuous across the active-set boundary; it was\nderived from the full six-anchor maximum, not a terminal ansatz.\n\n## 5. Return terminal and the retained old first-r query\n\nThe feasible return parameter set itself implies M>=2B^2>=8 and\nepsilon>0. Epsilon=0 and small M outside this set are not conclusions\nof the return theorem. Thus t_D is below w.\n   The t_Q affine value is (M-1)(<q,v_R>-Q)<0.\n4. y and 0 have value zero, below the positive w value.\n5. The exact identities d(2A_j+d)=A_j^2 and\n2A_j+d=lambda A_j match accepted042's primitive join. The full\nparent remainder is the original-coordinate sum of the two child\nremainders with weights 1 and kappa. Define\n\n omega=(-2w_0,...,-2w_(r-1),2s)^T,\n D_prim=diag(w_t(2-w_t) for t<r,-s(s+2)),\n M_prim=D_prim+sym(L_flow T), sym(K)=(K+K^T)/2.\n\nAccepted042 gives, under L_flow^T e=omega,\n\n V_w+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij\n           =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.                  (3)\n\nThe pairing is the trace matrix pairing. It is an identity on the full\nformal gradient space, not only on an actual trajectory. The x_0 row\nof this finite primitive identity is zero; no optimum row is used to\nsupply a missing initial-point diagonal. All internal function values\ncancel by the displayed full flow. A zero physical step removes no\nformal gradient and imposes no equality of formal coordinates.\n\n### 3.1 Complete primitive q_m arrays\n\nStart q_0 with the zero array. At the join from q_j to q_(j+1), let\nA_j=lambda^j, d=(lambda-2)A_j, kappa=lambda^2. Let p be the physical\nbridge query, z the parent terminal, and t range over EVERY right-child\nnonterminal query with its actual step w_t. Embed the entire left array\nwith factor 1 and the entire right array with factor kappa, then add\n\n Lambda_(t,p) += (kappa-1)w_t,\n Lambda_(z,p) += kappa-1,\n Lambda_(p,z) += (kappa-1)d,\n Lambda_(t,z) += (kappa-1)w_t.                            (4)\n\nOverlapping contributions add; all unlisted entries are zero. This\ndefines the full array without executing the recurrence. All signs\nare nonnegative. The exact identities d(2A_j+d)=A_j^2 and\n2A_j+d=lambda A_j match accepted042's primitive join. The full\nparent remainder is the original-coordinate sum of the two child\nremainders with weights 1 and kappa. Starting with zero gives\nM_prim=0 for EVERY actual q_m, on ALL 2^m original gradient coordinates.\nThe sum is A_m-1 and the length 2^m-1 by symbolic induction.\n\n### 3.2 Complete objective p_m array, including the initial mixed term\n\nFor weights u_i>=0 of sum D=1+2sum w define\n\n J_(w,u)=||z_0||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)-2D f_r\n                                 -||z_0-sum_i u_i g_i||^2. (5)\n\nHere z_0 is the ACTUAL local starting position relative to the same\nglobal minimizer. If the module occurs later in a physical word,\nz_0=x_0 minus the full previous weighted gradient displacement; it\nis not a new origin or an independently chosen initial point.\n\nFor p_m write p for the query after q_m and N for its terminal. Set\n\n u=(q_m,A+1,zeta_m), D=zeta_m^2=2A+zeta_m.\n\nAccepted042's dominant join with EMPTY dominant right child is the\nfull identity\n\n J_(p_m,u)=V_(q_m)-4A Q_(N,p).                           (6)\n\nTherefore the entire objective array at level 1/D consists of:\n\n - each q_m finite multiplier divided by D;\n - multiplier 4A/D on the ordered finite pair (N,p);\n - multiplier 2u_i/D on each original pair (i,star).\n\nEvery multiplier is nonnegative. At each internal q_m query, the\nfinite-child flow -2(q_m)_i/D is cancelled by 2u_i/D. At p the\nfull finite flow is [2(A-1)-4A+2(A+1)]/D=0. At N it is\n(4A+2zeta_m)/D=2. Thus every original function value cancels.\nBy (3),(6), the entire formal remainder is exactly\n\n (1/D)||z_0||^2-2f_N+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij\n                 =||z_0-sum_i u_i g_i||^2/D.             (7)\n\nThis is PSD on ALL original gradients and the free local starting\nvector. When embedded later, its square retains all original initial\nmixed terms and all past gradients through the actual z_0. Its\nsingular directions are retained, not quotiented out. The matching\nwhole-word Huber from accepted b98 yields W_(2^m)(p_m)=1/D.\n\n### 3.3 A direct type counterexample\n\nThe objective completion p_m cannot be used as a primitive block.\nFor m>=1, on the actual unit quadratic f(x)=x^2/2, x_0=1, the\nendpoint factor of q_m is -1/A. At m=1 it is 1-sqrt(2)=-1/lambda.\nIf it is -1/A_j for a child, the next join gives\n(-1/A_j)^2[-(lambda-2)A_j]=-1/(lambda A_j).\nThis is an exact symbolic induction, with no executed trajectory.\n\nThe cap of p_m has factor -e_m, so its actual endpoint is 1/zeta_m.\nOn a unit-quadratic trajectory, summing\nw_t(2-w_t)x_t^2=x_t^2-x_(t+1)^2 gives\n\n V_w=1-(1+sum w_t)^2 x_r^2.\n\nFor p_m, 1+sum p_m=A+e_m+1=(zeta_m^2+1)/2, hence\n\n V_(p_m)=1-(zeta_m^2+1)^2/(4zeta_m^2)\n                   =-(zeta_m^2-1)^2/(4zeta_m^2)<0.       (8)\n\nThis is an actual smooth-convex transcript, not a formal array test.\nIt excludes EVERY primitive certificate for this exact objective\nmodule. The objective certificate (7) remains valid. This type\nfailure prevents a migration which simply puts p_m into the primitive\nslots of accepted042. Equations (5)--(7) show exactly what its actual\nobjective resource is; no virtual Qhat<=0 is assumed.\n\n## 4. Original benchmark at the actual physical horizons\n\nUse ONLY the ORIGINAL ALL-split recurrences\n\n C_0=B_0=1,\n C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),\n Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. Accepted legal screens, every original cut, and full trajectories\n\nFor ANY actual nonnegative word h of length N, accepted b98 supplies\n\n W_N(h)>=1/(1+2sum h),\n W_N(h)>=(h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)] for EVERY j,    (19)\n\nwhere P_j=sum_(t<j)h_t, T_j=sum_(t>j)h_t and (v)_+=max(v,0).\nThe total sum in each family is (D_family-1)/2. For a selected physical step h_j>1\nput c=1/(1+P_j), d=c(h_j-1)/(1+2T_j), and use the SINGLE static\nasymmetric-Huber function\n\n F_(c,d)(x)=-d*x-d^2/2 for x<=-d,\n            x^2/2      for -d<=x<=c,\n            c*x-c^2/2 for x>=c. At every original suffix\nindex i>=j+1,\n\n x_i=-d(1+2T_j)+d sum_(j<t<i)h_t<=-d,\n g_i=-d, f_i=-d*x_i-d^2/2.\n\nThe terminal payoff is d^2(1+2T_j), exactly (19). Known whole-word quadratic screens\n\nFor every theta in [0,1], f(x)=theta*x^2/2 is a legal normalized\nquadratic, with payoff theta times the square of the WHOLE physical\nproduct. This pays the initial-anchor\n  price at A=1/2; a large-A bound has not been substituted.\n\nAll eight anchors, including z and the FIRST r query, have now been checked.\nThe convex combination w+theta*ER of w,tR is active and\nx_n=v+w+theta*ER is a subgradient of H4 at v. Thus v is the UNIQUE\nACTUAL terminal gradient, with exact normalized value\n\n    V4=2F4(x_n)\n      =Q*[E^2-(E*T-2(M-1))^2/N4],\n    V4>=(1/2)E^2 Q=E^2/(28M^2).                         (11)\n\nFor the lower bound,0<E*T-2(M-1)<E*T and T^2/N4<1/2.\nIn particular the exact formula has no omitted return-memory anchor.\nThe old function's unqueried value need not be0 throughout the stated\nE interval; it is irrelevant to the fixed completion and is not asserted.\n\nThe complete minimum set is conv{0,y,z,w}, minimum0. These are exactly\nthe active affine slopes at v=0, all other offsets being positive.\nAll nonzero vertices have negative first coordinate. (12)\n\nThis is an analytical limit of the printed formula, not a numerical fit.\nAll guards were order-independent and hold at every physical query.\n\n### Same-word K_n screen\n\nWe first prove a uniform small ratio for the ordinary Chebyshev cuts.\nIn decreasing order write h_k=csc^2(k*theta), theta=pi/(2N+1).\nConcavity of sin gives h_l>=h_k*(k/l)^2 for l>=k. Repeating the preceding full-word\nfactor calculation gives\n\n    sup_(z in Lens_n)Gap_h(z)<=2*Kc^24*4^8/T0=O(1/M)=o(V4).\n\nThis does not upper-bound the FULL schedule-dependent complex legal region.\n\n### A complete comparison with the homogeneous two-block/last-cut policy\n\nThis comparison includes EVERY original choice j before the LAST step,\nincluding ordinary entries, not only the three named bridge choices.\nThe precisely scoped reference is: fix p=e1/M0', use ONE selected\nbridge with excess A'*M0', run all remaining preterminal steps as one\nconstant-gradient block of mass M1'-1, and require its last proximal\nheight0 with homogeneous second slope. Independent affine heights do NOT remove the return debt\n\nThis is an exact scoped failure of a proposed THIRD repair, not a third\nattained construction and not a general impossibility theorem.\n\nSuppose ANY fixed max-affine function has two DISTINCT slopes q,r with\nindependently fixed offsets o_q,o_r. If C=0 they directly contradict one another.\nConsequently every such fixed-two-plane return NECESSARILY obeys\n\n    D*C >= M2*M3. The complete\nrelaxed matrix and exact constant-slack expenditure are derived first.\nThree explicit changed supports then fail in precisely stated original\ndirections. Prepaying the previously missing orientations does not by\nitself pay the whole gradient matrix. No general support-changing\nimpossibility or unrestricted W upper/lower bound is asserted.\n\n## 1. Target, fixed word, and authority\n\nFor a nonnegative word h of physical length n, W_n(h) is the supremum of\n2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over all finite dimensions,\nconvex differentiable L-smooth objectives with an attained minimum, L>0,\nand x_0!=x_star, under x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R.\nThe original all-split benchmark is\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nEvery split is an original integer split. The negative route needs a full\nall-function upper on unbounded actual horizons with a fixed relative\nimprovement. A failed dual is not a primal function or a W conclusion.\n\nFix ONE enclosing m>=1536, tau=1/4, and set\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    Hword_0=(), Hword_(j+1)=(Hword_j,1+a*c^j,Hword_j),\n    S_j=sum Hword_j, A_j=1+S_j, d_j=a*c^j, D_j=2A_j-1,\n    n_j=2^j-1, 0<=j<=m.\n\nEvery child keeps these SAME enclosing parameters. Hword_m is the\noriginal H_(m,1/4), with no altered entry or horizon reset. Epsilon=0 is\nthe Silver diagnostic, never a substitute for the actual positive epsilon.\nAll recurrences below are finite definitions/identities; none was executed.\n\nAccepted inputs are fact1252b8823bb5a290 (original normalized interpolation,\nfull formal space and certificate convention), fact04219554e41d9750\nSections10–11 (primitive history and full-space composition conventions),\nand fact5fd396eba75651e7 Section10 ONLY for B_(n_m)/D_m<237/250.\nThe latter's witness-family optimum is not an upper bound for W.\nFull findings1a79c9fd34296d77 and aed1ed37a3df33d4 have identical evidence\nand were read in full, together with89a0ae3ddc4fe35a,\nelaboration971c9cb839662d46 and guidancef51ffc777372c82f.\nThey are awareness, never predecessors. All unverified identities used\nhere are derived in this ONE joint draft, rather than adopted as facts.\n\nThe operational advertised level for all three laws is\n\n    zeta_0=101/100, q_j=zeta_0/D_j.\n\nTheir zero-initial-point failures actually apply to every real q.\nIncoming optimum multipliers are written xiCert_i, distinct from the\nproject's unrelated complex-prefix notation xi_i.\n\n## 2. Full rows, exact flow and the complete relaxed matrix\n\nNormalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Retain ALL independent original\nformal vectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n, including bridge and terminal gradients.\nFor any physical word put\n\n    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n    Ppos_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, S=sum h, D=1+2S, h_n=0,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nAll actual rows satisfy Q_ij<=0 by fact1252b8823bb5a290.\nLet K_ij be finite multipliers (K_ii=0), nu_i the i->star multiplier,\nand xiCert_i the star->i multiplier. All FINAL multipliers are required\nnonnegative. Exact value cancellation is\n\n    sum_j K_ij-sum_j K_ji+nu_i-xiCert_i=2*1_(i=n).       (1)\n\nWrite out_i=nu_i+sum_j K_ij. The entire original certificate is\n\n    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)K_ij Q_ij\n                    +sum_i nu_i Q_(i,star)\n                    +sum_i xiCert_i Q_(star,i)\n        =q||x_0||^2-sum_i nu_i<x_0,g_i>+Cgrad(g),        (2)\n\nwhere Cgrad(g)=sum_(i,j)Cgrad_ij<g_i,g_j> and\n\n    Cgrad_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i sum_(v>i)K_iv,\n    2Cgrad_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j sum_(v>j)K_iv\n                         +h_i[nu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. (3)\n\nThe full matrix is [[q,-nu^T/2],[-nu/2,Cgrad]]. Incoming optimum rows\nare present: their diagonal contribution is included through (1), while\nthey create no x_0 mixed term. Half the incident mass at i equals\nout_i-1_(i=n). Forward position terms subtract h_i K_iv from the source\ndiagonal; difference squares and both prefix position terms give (3).\nThis derivation covers nonzero xiCert and every original off-diagonal.\n\nFor q>0, completing the initial-point square proves full PSD equivalent to\n\n    Cgrad-(nu nu^T)/(4q) >=0 on ALL gradient coordinates. (4)\n\nNo selected-plane, child-quotient or diagonal positivity replaces (4).\nIf this full matrix is PSD, then every actual legal transcript satisfies\nq||x_0||^2-2f_n>=M(q)>=0 because all multiplied Q rows are nonpositive.\nThus full PSD is a sufficient all-function certificate for W_n(h)<=q.\nNo such sufficiency is inferred for any failed array below.\n\n## 3. Exact constant-slack expenditure; no forced contact\n\nLet e be the all-ones gradient-index vector, csum=Cgrad e, and define\n\n    X=sum_i xiCert_i,\n    E=sum_i(1+Ppos_i)xiCert_i,\n    Hprice=sum_i(2S-Ppos_i)xiCert_i=D X-E,\n    delta=zeta-1>0, q=zeta/D.\n\nSumming (1) gives sum nu_i=2+X. Its position-weighted sum gives\nsum K_ij(Ppos_i-Ppos_j)=2S-sum(nu_i-xiCert_i)Ppos_i.\nExpanding the common-gradient polynomial consequently yields\n\n    e^T Cgrad e=D+E.                                  (5)\n\nUse the explicit one-sided Huber function Hub_1(z), equal to zero for\nz<=0, z^2/2 on [0,1], and z-1/2 for z>=1. It is convex and 1-smooth.\nStarting at D gives x_i=D-Ppos_i>=S+1>=1 at every query.\nThe actual scale-D Huber transcript is x_0=D, g_i=1,\nf_i=D-Ppos_i-1/2. Its finite and outgoing optimum rows have residual\nzero; its incoming residual is -(2S-Ppos_i). Hence exactly\n\n    M(D,e)=delta D-Hprice.                            (6)\n\nNecessary WHOLE-word Huber expenditure is Hprice<=delta D.\nSince Ppos_i<=S, Hprice>=S X and X<=delta D/S when S>0.\nThis is not a renewable allowance for each child or outgoing row.\n\nThe full common-gradient/initial-point restriction is\n\n    M(x_0=r,g=t e)=q r^2-(2+X)r t+(D+E)t^2.            (7)\n\nIts exact PSD condition is\n\n    zeta >= D(2+X)^2/[4(D+E)].                         (8)\n\nIt retains the changed outgoing mass2+X and can be stronger than (6).\n\nThe complete common-gradient derivative is\n\n    2(csum)_i=2h_i+xiCert_i+h_i sum_(t>i)xiCert_t\n       +(1+Ppos_i)nu_i+sum_j K_ij(Ppos_i-Ppos_j). Adding later\noutgoing optimum contributions makes that sum 2+sum_(t>i)xiCert_t;\nthe incoming square contributes xiCert_i. This proves (9), including\nthe original terminal coordinate h_n=0.\n\nFor arbitrary incoming support put\n\n    Acommon=[[q,-(2+X)/2],[-(2+X)/2,D+E]],\n    DeltaCommon=q(D+E)-(2+X)^2/4.\n\nIf DeltaCommon>0, write the scalar gradient vector uniquely as t e+v,\ne^T v=0. Its coupling to (x_0,t) is\nl(v)=(-nu^T v/2,csum^T v). Exact expenditure on an arbitrary direction is\n\n    Price(v)=l(v)^T Acommon^(-1)l(v)\n      =[(D+E)(nu^T v)^2/4\n          -(2+X)(nu^T v)(csum^T v)/2\n          +q(csum^T v)^2]/DeltaCommon.                (10)\n\nUnder DeltaCommon>0, full original-space PSD is equivalent to the stated\npositive definiteness of Acommon\nand v^T Cgrad v>=Price(v) for EVERY v in e-perp. This is an invertible\ndecomposition retaining all coordinates, not a selected quotient theorem.\nIf DeltaCommon=0, the coupling must annihilate Acommon's kernel and the\nsemidefinite Schur condition remains necessary; no singular direction is\nsilently removed.\n\nFor zero incoming mass let\n\n    dcontact=csum-D nu/2, e^T dcontact=0.\n\nThen DeltaCommon=delta and (10) is exactly\n\n    Price(v)=(csum^T v)^2/D\n                       +(dcontact^T v)^2/[D(zeta-1)]. (11)\n\nThus relaxed q does NOT force dcontact=0. The contact-defect square spends\n1/[D(zeta-1)] on the entire transverse space. At the tight level zeta=1\nthe coupling would force zero defect; importing that kernel at larger q\nwould improperly discard allowed constructions. A fixed negative direction\nof Cgrad at x_0=0 is nevertheless unaffected by any q.\n\nEquations(1)–(11) are full necessary conditions and exact square-completion\nidentities, jointly UNVERIFIED here. Explicit baseline, actual innovation plane and calibrations\n\nAll coefficients required to make the three changed laws concrete are\ngiven here and rederived within this draft.\n\n### 4.1 Complete finite source\n\nFor a depth-j child terminal z define the primitive history\n\n    V_j=2sum_(i<z)h_i(f_i-f_z)\n         +sum_(i<z)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n         -A_j(A_j-1)||g_z||^2.\n\nStart Lambda^0=0. Target, fixed word, and authority\n\nFor a nonnegative word h of physical length n, W_n(h) is the supremum of\n2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over all finite dimensions,\nconvex differentiable L-smooth objectives with an attained minimum, L>0,\nand x_0!=x_star, under x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R.\nThe original all-split benchmark is\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nEvery split is an original integer split. The negative route needs a full\nall-function upper on unbounded actual horizons with a fixed relative\nimprovement. A failed dual is not a primal function or a W conclusion.\n\nFix ONE enclosing m>=1536, tau=1/4, and set\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    Hword_0=(), Hword_(j+1)=(Hword_j,1+a*c^j,Hword_j),\n    S_j=sum Hword_j, A_j=1+S_j, d_j=a*c^j, D_j=2A_j-1,\n    n_j=2^j-1, 0<=j<=m.\n\nEvery child keeps these SAME enclosing parameters. Epsilon=0 is\nthe Silver diagnostic, never a substitute for the actual positive epsilon.\nAll recurrences below are finite definitions/identities; none was executed.\n\nAccepted inputs are fact1252b8823bb5a290 (original normalized interpolation,\nfull formal space and certificate convention), fact04219554e41d9750\nSections10–11 (primitive history and full-space composition conventions),\nand fact5fd396eba75651e7 Section10 ONLY for B_(n_m)/D_m<237/250.\nThe latter's witness-family optimum is not an upper bound for W.\nFull findings1a79c9fd34296d77 and aed1ed37a3df33d4 have identical evidence\nand were read in full, together with89a0ae3ddc4fe35a,\nelaboration971c9cb839662d46 and guidancef51ffc777372c82f.\nThey are awareness, never predecessors. All unverified identities used\nhere are derived in this ONE joint draft, rather than adopted as facts.\n\nThe operational advertised level for all three laws is\n\n    zeta_0=101/100, q_j=zeta_0/D_j.\n\nTheir zero-initial-point failures actually apply to every real q.\nIncoming optimum multipliers are written xiCert_i, distinct from the\nproject's unrelated complex-prefix notation xi_i.\n\n## 2. All FINAL multipliers are required\nnonnegative. Exact value cancellation is\n\n    sum_j K_ij-sum_j K_ji+nu_i-xiCert_i=2*1_(i=n).       (1)\n\nWrite out_i=nu_i+sum_j K_ij. The entire original certificate is\n\n    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)K_ij Q_ij\n                    +sum_i nu_i Q_(i,star)\n                    +sum_i xiCert_i Q_(star,i)\n        =q||x_0||^2-sum_i nu_i<x_0,g_i>+Cgrad(g),        (2)\n\nwhere Cgrad(g)=sum_(i,j)Cgrad_ij<g_i,g_j> and\n\n    Cgrad_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i sum_(v>i)K_iv,\n    2Cgrad_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j sum_(v>j)K_iv\n                         +h_i[nu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. Half the incident mass at i equals\nout_i-1_(i=n). Forward position terms subtract h_i K_iv from the source\ndiagonal; difference squares and both prefix position terms give (3).\nThis derivation covers nonzero xiCert and every original off-diagonal.\n\nFor q>0, completing the initial-point square proves full PSD equivalent to\n\n    Cgrad-(nu nu^T)/(4q) >=0 on ALL gradient coordinates. (4)\n\nNo selected-plane, child-quotient or diagonal positivity replaces (4).\nIf this full matrix is PSD, then every actual legal transcript satisfies\nq||x_0||^2-2f_n>=M(q)>=0 because all multiplied Q rows are nonpositive.\nThus full PSD is a sufficient all-function certificate for W_n(h)<=q.\nNo such sufficiency is inferred for any failed array below.\n\n## 3. This proves (9), including\nthe original terminal coordinate h_n=0.\n\nFor arbitrary incoming support put\n\n    Acommon=[[q,-(2+X)/2],[-(2+X)/2,D+E]],\n    DeltaCommon=q(D+E)-(2+X)^2/4.\n\nIf DeltaCommon>0, write the scalar gradient vector uniquely as t e+v,\ne^T v=0. Explicit baseline, actual innovation plane and calibrations\n\nAll coefficients required to make the three changed laws concrete are\ngiven here and rederived within this draft.\n\n### 4.1 Complete finite source\n\nFor a depth-j child terminal z define the primitive history\n\n    V_j=2sum_(i<z)h_i(f_i-f_z)\n         +sum_(i<z)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n         -A_j(A_j-1)||g_z||^2.\n\nStart Lambda^0=0. No unverified intermediate is an accepted predecessor. The definitions, entire matrix, necessary budget and complete baseline are followed by this law's full coefficients, new directions, calibration and original-space failure:\n\n# Advertised objective slack and three changed finite/optimum supports\n\nJOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT — UNVERIFIED. Worker max, allocated invocation74\nafter completed baseline73, attempted ceiling75. This is research evidence,\nnot a fact. No formal gate, mathematical program, numerical replay,\ntrajectory/recurrence execution, optimizer or subagent was used.\n\nThe interface is q=zeta/D, 1<zeta<250/237. Exact Huber contact and zero\nincoming optimum rows are NOT imposed on the changed laws. The complete\nrelaxed matrix and exact constant-slack expenditure are derived first.\nThree explicit changed supports then fail in precisely stated original\ndirections. Prepaying the previously missing orientations does not by\nitself pay the whole gradient matrix. No general support-changing\nimpossibility or unrestricted W upper/lower bound is asserted.\n\n## 1. Target, fixed word, and authority\n\nFor a nonnegative word h of physical length n, W_n(h) is the supremum of\n2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over all finite dimensions,\nconvex differentiable L-smooth objectives with an attained minimum, L>0,\nand x_0!=x_star, under x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R.\nThe original all-split benchmark is\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nEvery split is an original integer split. The negative route needs a full\nall-function upper on unbounded actual horizons with a fixed relative\nimprovement. A failed dual is not a primal function or a W conclusion.\n\nFix ONE enclosing m>=1536, tau=1/4, and set\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    Hword_0=(), Hword_(j+1)=(Hword_j,1+a*c^j,Hword_j),\n    S_j=sum Hword_j, A_j=1+S_j, d_j=a*c^j, D_j=2A_j-1,\n    n_j=2^j-1, 0<=j<=m.\n\nEvery child keeps these SAME enclosing parameters. Hword_m is the\noriginal H_(m,1/4), with no altered entry or horizon reset. Epsilon=0 is\nthe Silver diagnostic, never a substitute for the actual positive epsilon.\nAll recurrences below are finite definitions/identities; none was executed.\n\nAccepted inputs are fact1252b8823bb5a290 (original normalized interpolation,\nfull formal space and certificate convention), fact04219554e41d9750\nSections10–11 (primitive history and full-space composition conventions),\nand fact5fd396eba75651e7 Section10 ONLY for B_(n_m)/D_m<237/250.\nThe latter's witness-family optimum is not an upper bound for W.\nFull findings1a79c9fd34296d77 and aed1ed37a3df33d4 have identical evidence\nand were read in full, together with89a0ae3ddc4fe35a,\nelaboration971c9cb839662d46 and guidancef51ffc777372c82f.\nThey are awareness, never predecessors. All unverified identities used\nhere are derived in this ONE joint draft, rather than adopted as facts.\n\nThe operational advertised level for all three laws is\n\n    zeta_0=101/100, q_j=zeta_0/D_j.\n\nTheir zero-initial-point failures actually apply to every real q.\nIncoming optimum multipliers are written xiCert_i, distinct from the\nproject's unrelated complex-prefix notation xi_i.\n\n## 2. Full rows, exact flow and the complete relaxed matrix\n\nNormalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Retain ALL independent original\nformal vectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n, including bridge and terminal gradients.\nFor any physical word put\n\n    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n    Ppos_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, S=sum h, D=1+2S, h_n=0,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nAll actual rows satisfy Q_ij<=0 by fact1252b8823bb5a290.\nLet K_ij be finite multipliers (K_ii=0), nu_i the i->star multiplier,\nand xiCert_i the star->i multiplier. All FINAL multipliers are required\nnonnegative. Exact value cancellation is\n\n    sum_j K_ij-sum_j K_ji+nu_i-xiCert_i=2*1_(i=n).       (1)\n\nWrite out_i=nu_i+sum_j K_ij. The entire original certificate is\n\n    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)K_ij Q_ij\n                    +sum_i nu_i Q_(i,star)\n                    +sum_i xiCert_i Q_(star,i)\n        =q||x_0||^2-sum_i nu_i<x_0,g_i>+Cgrad(g),        (2)\n\nwhere Cgrad(g)=sum_(i,j)Cgrad_ij<g_i,g_j> and\n\n    Cgrad_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i sum_(v>i)K_iv,\n    2Cgrad_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j sum_(v>j)K_iv\n                         +h_i[nu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. (3)\n\nThe full matrix is [[q,-nu^T/2],[-nu/2,Cgrad]]. Incoming optimum rows\nare present: their diagonal contribution is included through (1), while\nthey create no x_0 mixed term. Half the incident mass at i equals\nout_i-1_(i=n). Forward position terms subtract h_i K_iv from the source\ndiagonal; difference squares and both prefix position terms give (3).\nThis derivation covers nonzero xiCert and every original off-diagonal.\n\nFor q>0, completing the initial-point square proves full PSD equivalent to\n\n    Cgrad-(nu nu^T)/(4q) >=0 on ALL gradient coordinates. (4)\n\nNo selected-plane, child-quotient or diagonal positivity replaces (4).\nIf this full matrix is PSD, then every actual legal transcript satisfies\nq||x_0||^2-2f_n>=M(q)>=0 because all multiplied Q rows are nonpositive.\nThus full PSD is a sufficient all-function certificate for W_n(h)<=q.\nNo such sufficiency is inferred for any failed array below.\n\n## 3. Exact constant-slack expenditure; no forced contact\n\nLet e be the all-ones gradient-index vector, csum=Cgrad e, and define\n\n    X=sum_i xiCert_i,\n    E=sum_i(1+Ppos_i)xiCert_i,\n    Hprice=sum_i(2S-Ppos_i)xiCert_i=D X-E,\n    delta=zeta-1>0, q=zeta/D.\n\nSumming (1) gives sum nu_i=2+X. Its position-weighted sum gives\nsum K_ij(Ppos_i-Ppos_j)=2S-sum(nu_i-xiCert_i)Ppos_i.\nExpanding the common-gradient polynomial consequently yields\n\n    e^T Cgrad e=D+E.                                  (5)\n\nUse the explicit one-sided Huber function Hub_1(z), equal to zero for\nz<=0, z^2/2 on [0,1], and z-1/2 for z>=1. It is convex and 1-smooth.\nStarting at D gives x_i=D-Ppos_i>=S+1>=1 at every query.\nThe actual scale-D Huber transcript is x_0=D, g_i=1,\nf_i=D-Ppos_i-1/2. Its finite and outgoing optimum rows have residual\nzero; its incoming residual is -(2S-Ppos_i). Hence exactly\n\n    M(D,e)=delta D-Hprice.                            (6)\n\nNecessary WHOLE-word Huber expenditure is Hprice<=delta D.\nSince Ppos_i<=S, Hprice>=S X and X<=delta D/S when S>0.\nThis is not a renewable allowance for each child or outgoing row.\n\nThe full common-gradient/initial-point restriction is\n\n    M(x_0=r,g=t e)=q r^2-(2+X)r t+(D+E)t^2.            (7)\n\nIts exact PSD condition is\n\n    zeta >= D(2+X)^2/[4(D+E)].                         (8)\n\nIt retains the changed outgoing mass2+X and can be stronger than (6).\n\nThe complete common-gradient derivative is\n\n    2(csum)_i=2h_i+xiCert_i+h_i sum_(t>i)xiCert_t\n       +(1+Ppos_i)nu_i+sum_j K_ij(Ppos_i-Ppos_j).       (9)\n\nFor finite rows the source derivative is the last term. Their physical\nposition derivative is h_i times later finite-node flow. Adding later\noutgoing optimum contributions makes that sum 2+sum_(t>i)xiCert_t;\nthe incoming square contributes xiCert_i. This proves (9), including\nthe original terminal coordinate h_n=0.\n\nFor arbitrary incoming support put\n\n    Acommon=[[q,-(2+X)/2],[-(2+X)/2,D+E]],\n    DeltaCommon=q(D+E)-(2+X)^2/4.\n\nIf DeltaCommon>0, write the scalar gradient vector uniquely as t e+v,\ne^T v=0. Its coupling to (x_0,t) is\nl(v)=(-nu^T v/2,csum^T v). Exact expenditure on an arbitrary direction is\n\n    Price(v)=l(v)^T Acommon^(-1)l(v)\n      =[(D+E)(nu^T v)^2/4\n          -(2+X)(nu^T v)(csum^T v)/2\n          +q(csum^T v)^2]/DeltaCommon.                (10)\n\nUnder DeltaCommon>0, full original-space PSD is equivalent to the stated\npositive definiteness of Acommon\nand v^T Cgrad v>=Price(v) for EVERY v in e-perp. This is an invertible\ndecomposition retaining all coordinates, not a selected quotient theorem.\nIf DeltaCommon=0, the coupling must annihilate Acommon's kernel and the\nsemidefinite Schur condition remains necessary; no singular direction is\nsilently removed.\n\nFor zero incoming mass let\n\n    dcontact=csum-D nu/2, e^T dcontact=0.\n\nThen DeltaCommon=delta and (10) is exactly\n\n    Price(v)=(csum^T v)^2/D\n                       +(dcontact^T v)^2/[D(zeta-1)]. (11)\n\nThus relaxed q does NOT force dcontact=0. The contact-defect square spends\n1/[D(zeta-1)] on the entire transverse space. At the tight level zeta=1\nthe coupling would force zero defect; importing that kernel at larger q\nwould improperly discard allowed constructions. A fixed negative direction\nof Cgrad at x_0=0 is nevertheless unaffected by any q.\n\nEquations(1)–(11) are full necessary conditions and exact square-completion\nidentities, jointly UNVERIFIED here. They are not an accepted intermediate\nor an unrestricted W upper.\n\n## 4. Explicit baseline, actual innovation plane and calibrations\n\nAll coefficients required to make the three changed laws concrete are\ngiven here and rederived within this draft.\n\n### 4.1 Complete finite source\n\nFor a depth-j child terminal z define the primitive history\n\n    V_j=2sum_(i<z)h_i(f_i-f_z)\n         +sum_(i<z)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n         -A_j(A_j-1)||g_z||^2.\n\nStart Lambda^0=0. At Hword_(j+1)=(Hword_j,1+d_j,Hword_j), with bridge\np and terminal z, embed Lambda^j on the left with weight1 and on the\nright with weight k_j. Add precisely\n\n    u_j sum_(i right,i<z)h_i Q_(i,p)\n     +v_j sum_(i right,i<z)h_i Q_(i,z)\n     +w_j Q_(z,p)+z_j Q_(p,z),\n    u_j=2lambda,\n    v_j=[u_j d_j+2(A_j+d_j)]/A_j,\n    k_j=1+(u_j+v_j)/2,\n    z_j=2A_j,\n    w_j=4A_j+2d_j-u_j(A_j-1).                         (12)\n\nAll unlisted entries are zero; repeated original rows are summed.\nThe identities u_j+v_j=2(k_j-1) and\nu_j(A_j-1)+w_j-z_j=2(A_j+d_j) give nonterminal divergence -2h_i and\nterminal divergence2(A_(j+1)-1). Put\nPsrc_j=V_j+sum_(i!=v)Lambda^j_iv Q_iv.\nWith t_j=d_j/A_j, t_star=1/lambda,\nr=sum_(i right,i<z)h_i g_i, Y=r-(A_j-1)g_z, Z=g_p-g_z,\nthe full identity is\n\n    Psrc_(j+1)=Psrc_j^left+k_j Psrc_j^right\n       +(t_star-t_j)[(lambda+1)||Y||^2\n              -2lambda A_j<Y,Z>+A_j^2(2+t_star+t_j)||Z||^2]. (13)\n\nFor an independent coefficient audit, the four new unweighted gradient\npolynomials, in (12)'s order, are\n\n    d_j<r,g_p>+||r||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2\n                                      +(A_j-1)||g_p||^2/2;\n    -||r||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2\n                                      +(A_j-1)||g_z||^2/2-<r,g_z>;\n    d_j<g_z,g_p>+<g_z,r>+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2;\n    -(d_j+1/2)||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2/2-<g_p,r>-<g_p,g_z>.\n\nTheir weighted r^2,r*g_p,r*g_z coefficients are\n(u_j-v_j)/2, u_j d_j-z_j, w_j-v_j. The other two diagonals after V are\n\n    A_j(A_j-1)-d_j-d_j^2+[u_j(A_j-1)+w_j]/2-(d_j+1/2)z_j,\n    -A_(j+1)(A_(j+1)-1)+k_j A_j(A_j-1)\n                                   +v_j(A_j-1)/2+(w_j+z_j)/2.\n\nSubstitution yields (13) on ALL original gradients. Its bracket is\npositive definite: the determinant divided by A_j^2 is\n(lambda+1)(2+t_star+t_j)-lambda^2>=lambda>0.\n\nFinite summation of the actual physical word gives\n\n    v_h=a/(c-2), 0<v_h<1,\n    A_j=v_h c^j+(1-v_h)2^j,\n    t_j=(c-2)/[1+(1/v_h-1)(2/c)^j].                   (14)\n\nSince c(c-2)>1>beta, the ratios increase. The first ones satisfy\nt_0<t_star, t_1<t_star, t_2>t_star, as witnessed by\n\n    lambda d_1-A_1=epsilon[-lambda+2/(lambda(1+epsilon))]<0,\n    c(lambda d_2-A_2)=epsilon[(lambda+4)\n                      -(4lambda+2)epsilon-(5lambda+2)epsilon^2]>0.\n\nFor the last sign use 12/5<lambda<5/2 and epsilon<=1/6144:\nthe positive constant exceeds6, while both removed terms together are\nless than1. Thus all later t_j exceed t_star. In (12), u,v,k,z are\npositive. Also w_j=2lambda+2(d_j-A_j/lambda)>0:\nw_0=4+2a, A_1<lambda gives w_1>2lambda-2, and j>=2 gives w_j>2lambda.\nAt epsilon=0, t_j=t_star and Psrc_j=0 identically by (13).\n\n### 4.2 Complete full objective array\n\nInitialize Kbase^0=0, mubase^0_0=2, xiCert=0. At a join j->j+1,\nk=n_j, use L_l=l for0<=l<=k, bridge p=L_k,\nR_i=k+1+i for0<=i<=k, terminal R_k. Set\n\n    A=A_j-1, d=1+d_j, V=3A+2d,\n    D=D_(j+1), D_c=D_j,\n    s_j=(A+d)/V, theta_j=1-s_j.\n\nDefine EVERY parent row by\n\n    Kbase_(L_l,L_v)=Lambda^j_lv/D,\n    Kbase_(R_i,R_v)=Kbase^j_iv,\n    Kbase_(R_i,p)=s_j mubase^j_i,\n    mubase_(L_l)=2h_l/D for l<k,\n    mubase_p=2s_j-2A/D,\n    mubase_(R_i)=theta_j mubase^j_i,\n    all other finite rows=0, all incoming optimum rows=0. (15)\n\nFor A=0, s_0=1/2; for A>0, 1/3<s_j<1/2. All final signs hold:\nthe bridge attachment is positive since\n(A+d)D-A(3A+2d)=A^2+4Ad+2d^2+A+d>0.\nSource flow is canceled by left2h_l/D attachments; its terminal flow2A/D\nis canceled by the bridge attachment and incoming right mass2s_j.\nEach right node restores its child flow because theta_j+s_j=1.\nThis proves (1), sum mubase=2 and exact value cancellation.\n\nLet Cbase^j be the gradient matrix,\nx_p=x_0-sum_(l<k)h_l g_(L_l), r_R=sum_i mubase^j_i g_(R_i),\nRchild_R=Cbase^j(g_R)-D_c||r_R||^2/4 and\nkappa_j=D_c-D theta_j^2. The full original identity is\n\n    Mbase_(j+1)(1/D)=Psrc_j(g_L)/D+Rchild_R\n       +||x_p+(A-D s_j)g_p-(D theta_j/2)r_R||^2/D\n       +(kappa_j/4)||r_R-2g_p||^2.                   (16)\n\nNeither Rchild_R nor kappa_j is presumed nonnegative on the actual word.\nFor checking, source plus left optimum rows gives Psrc_j/D and\n||x_p-(A+1)g_p||^2/D. Replacing each right optimum row by\ntheta_j Q_(R_i,star)+s_j Q_(R_i,p) adds\ns_j[-f_p+<g_(R_i),x_p-g_p>+||g_p||^2/2].\nAfter value cancellation the remaining polynomial is\n\n    ||x_p||^2/D+2(A/D-s_j)<x_p,g_p>\n      +(A^2/D+2s_j)||g_p||^2-theta_j<x_p,r_R>\n      +(d-s_j)<g_p,r_R>+D_c||r_R||^2/4.\n\nUsing s_j V=A+d completes it to (16). This keeps original x_0 and all\ninherited gradients without a child PSD assumption. It also proves\nbaseline exact contact csum=D mubase/2 by induction: source derivatives\nvanish at common gradients, the displayed square and difference vanish\nat x_0=D,g_i=1, and the inherited child remainder annihilates common\ngradients when the child has this contact identity.\n\nPut b=1+a, so 7/5<b<sqrt(2)<10/7. At depth1,\nKbase_1,0=1, mubase_0=mubase_1=1 and\n\n    Mbase_1(1/D_1)=||x_0-D_1(g_0+g_1)/2||^2/D_1\n                         +(3-2b)||g_0-g_1||^2/4,\n    Psrc_1=(2-b^2)||g_0-g_1||^2.                      (17)\n\nBoth are PSD. At depth2, d=2-epsilon, D_c>19/5, D<75/7,\ntheta_1<59/100 and D theta_1^2<15/4, so kappa_1>1/20.\nThese follow from b<10/7 and d>199/100:\ntheta_1=(2b+d)/(3b+2d) increases with b and decreases with d,\nand substitution at those rational endpoints gives theta_1<59/100.\nIdentity(16), positive Psrc_1, and (17)'s ENTIRE Schur remainder give\nfull actual H2 PSD, including ALL initial-point mixed directions.\n\nFor Silver let u_star=1/lambda, so d=1+u_star(A+1) and\nu_star^2+2u_star=1. Exact expansion gives\n\n    V^2 kappa_j=2(1-u_star)A^3+(6-8u_star)A^2\n       +(1+u_star)(8u_star-2)A+(1+u_star)^2(1-2u_star)>0. (18)\n\nEvery coefficient is positive because1/4<u_star<1/2.\nAt epsilon=0 sourcePsrc=0, so the empty square||x_0-g_0||^2 and (16)\nprove full Silver PSD inductively. This particular child PSD induction\nis justified: full child PSD with contact implies its entire Schur\nremainder Rchild_R>=0. No such induction is assumed on positive epsilon.\n\n### 4.3 Actual innovation plane and the sole decrement capacity\n\nSet\n\n    Wloc=A_2+d_2, Uloc=A_2+d_3, Tloc=Wloc+Uloc=A_3+d_3,\n    Edef=(lambda d_2-A_2)(A_2+d_2/lambda)>0,\n    delta3=t_3-t_star>0.\n\nIn a one-dimensional ORIGINAL formal-vector test, assign\ng_0,...,g_3=u; g_4,...,g_7=v0;\ng_8,...,g_11=w; g_12,...,g_15=0. Its displacement is\nWloc(u+w)+Uloc v0. The two-dimensional zero-displacement test plane N is\n\n    u=-w-(Uloc/Wloc)v0.                               (19)\n\nThis chooses test vectors; it identifies no independent formal coordinates.\nApplying (13) at the left H3, right H3 and their H4 join gives\n\n    P_N=Psrc_4|N=-Edef(u-v0)^2-k_3 Edef w^2\n       -delta3[(lambda+1)Wloc^2 w^2\n          -2lambda A_3 Wloc w v0+A_3^2(2+t_star+t_3)v0^2]<0 (20)\n\nat every nonzero point of N. Left H3 has Y=0,Z=u-v0, right H3 contributes\n-Edef*w^2, and the final bracket is positive definite by (13).\nPlace N in the FIRST actual H4 of Hword_r, all later gradients zero and\nx_0=0. Zero displacement and terminal make all higher source Y,Z zero\nwith left embedding weight1. At the final objective join all boundary\nand right variables vanish. Define the positive\nor zero upper comparison number\n\n    U_col_three=2A epsilon^2/(B+2)^2,\n    U_col_return=2A epsilon^2/(3B+4)^2,\n    rho_col_three=[B(B+2)^2/2](1-1/(2B^3)),\n    rho_col_return=(3/4)(3B+4)^2.\n\n## Exact named reference channels and original benchmark\n\nFor any h of length n, put s(h)=sum_(i<n)h_i, P_j=sum_(i<j)h_i,\nT_j=sum_(i>j)h_i, and (u)_+=max(u,0). The accepted single-cut channel is\n\n    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2s(h)),\n          max_(0<=j<n)(h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)]},\n\nwith the inner maximum omitted at n=0. Define the entire convex-quadratic\nchannel by\n\n    Q_n(h)=max_(0<=t<=1) t product_(i<n)(1-h_i t)^2.\n\nThis is the supremum of the normalized payoff over all finite-dimensional\nconvex quadratics with gradient Lipschitz constant at most L, with arbitrary\ninitial points and chosen minimizers, in the original normalization.\n\nFor n>=1 and a complex variable zeta=alpha+i beta, put\nPoly_0(zeta)=1, Poly_i(zeta)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t zeta),\nxi_i=Poly_i(zeta), gamma_i=zeta Poly_i(zeta), and xi_star=gamma_star=0.\nIdentify the complex plane with the real plane using <u,v>=Re(conj(u)v). On the index set\nI_n={star,0,...,n}, the weight of the directed edge j->i is\n\n    q_ij=<gamma_j,xi_i-xi_j>+||gamma_i-gamma_j||^2/2.\n\nLet ell_n be the maximum sum of these weights on directed simple paths\nn->star. Define Gap_h(zeta)=-2ell_n for zeta in the exact universal lens\n\n    Lens_n={alpha+i beta:\n       alpha-alpha^2-beta^2>=|beta|cot(pi/(n+2))},\n    L_lens_n(h)=sup_(zeta in Lens_n) Gap_h(zeta),\n    V_lens(h)=max{L_lens_n(h),1/(1+2s(h))}.\n\nThe meaning and legality of this transcript are those of accepted fact_id\ndfde2fa326c908bf; the reference is restricted to Lens_n. No upper\ncomparison with the full schedule-dependent complex legal region is made.\n\nUse the original all-split sequences, exactly as in accepted fact_id\nb98bb807d514e80d. The joins have real arguments x,y>=0, and the\nrecurrences apply for every integer n>=1:\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),\n    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,      lambda=1+sqrt(2),      Kc=cosh(2).\n\nEvery maximum here ranges over ALL integer splits, with C_n=S_circle(n)+1\nand B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 in that predecessor's notation.\n\nFor each integer N>=2^60, set\n\n    M=2N(N+1)+1, A=M^(1/12), B=M^(1/120), epsilon=M^(-1/3),\n    T_Cheb=(2N+1)^2=2M-1.\n\nLet Chi_N be the list of entries\nsec^2((2j-1)pi/[2(2N+1)]), 1<=j<=N. Let L_up be three consecutive\nincreasing copies of Chi_N and L_down three consecutive decreasing copies.\nUse L_0=L_up and L_i=L_down for every subsequent i. Thus the two base\nword lengths are 9N+3 and 12N+4. For every integer n>=9*2^60+3 define\nthe padded three-stage word by N=floor((n-3)/9), followed by inserting\nn-(9N+3) leading zero steps into that base word. For every integer\nn>=12*2^60+4 define the padded return word by N=floor((n-4)/12) and\ninsert n-(12N+4) leading zero steps. These are the two SPECIAL families\nin the conclusions below, with their respective N and parameters.\n\n## Conclusions\n\n(i) F_three and, on its additional parameter domain, F_return are finite\neverywhere, convex, continuously differentiable, and have least global\ngradient Lipschitz constant EXACTLY1. Each is the pointwise maximal legal\ncompletion of its complete specified old prefix. The exact maximum of\nall physical prefix conjugate quadratics is H_three or H_return, with\nsix or eight distinct affine anchors, respectively; the first r-query\nanchor t_R is retained in BOTH laws. Every old prefix point, value and\ngradient is preserved. The actual queried gradients are p,q,r for the\nthree-stage law and p,q,r,q for the return law, one constant gradient on\neach ordinary list and its following bridge query. Every signed ordered\ninterpolation residual, including every remote, optimum and terminal pair,\nin both orientations, is nonpositive for the resulting actual function.\nThe complete minimum sets and minimum values are\n\n    argmin F_three=conv{0,y,z},   min F_three=0,\n    argmin F_return=conv{0,y,z,w},   min F_return=0.\n\nOrigin is the unique closest minimizer to e_1, at distance1 in both cases.\n\n(ii) For the three-stage law set\n\n    L_stage=B+a, E_3=M(B+a)q+Mr,\n    D_3=||E_3||^2=2A[(B+a)^2-(B+a)+B/2],\n    b_3=Aa(epsilon-2/M), theta_3=(b_3)_+/D_3.\n\nThe actual terminal point, gradient and normalized payoff are exactly\n\n    x_n=z-M epsilon r,\n    grad F_three(x_n)=-M epsilon r-theta_3 E_3,\n    V_three=2F_three(x_n)\n       =AB epsilon^2-[Aa(epsilon-2/M)_+]^2/D_3,\n    AB epsilon^2(1-1/(2B^3))<=V_three<=AB epsilon^2.\n\nThis includes epsilon=0, epsilon=2/M when in range, M=2, and epsilon=1.\nFor the return law set\n\n    d_R=C+a, E_R=Mq+M d_R r,\n    Dhat_R=1-d_R+(B/2)d_R^2, D_R=||E_R||^2=2A Dhat_R,\n    b_R=2A[(B-1/(2M))epsilon-Ba/(2M)], theta_R=b_R/D_R.\n\nThe actual terminal quantities are exactly\n\n    x_n=w-M epsilon q,\n    grad F_return(x_n)=-M epsilon q-theta_R E_R,\n    V_return=2F_return(x_n)=2A epsilon^2-b_R^2/D_R,\n    2A epsilon^2(1-1/(2B))<=V_return<=2A epsilon^2.\n\nThey include the feasible boundary M epsilon=2B^2. The denominator\nbounds are 2B^3<=Dhat_R<=(9/2)B^3; the lower-point identity is exactly\n1-(2B+1)+(B/2)(2B+1)^2=2B^3+2B^2-3B/2.\nIn particular W_n(h_three)>=V_three and W_n(h_return)>=V_return.\n\n(iii) Every member of the narrowly defined collapsed completion class\nhas true closest initial radius1 and 0<=V_col(F_col)<=U_col for its word.\nOn the entire respective parameter domains the WEAK comparisons are\n\n    V_three>=rho_col_three U_col_three>=rho_col_three V_col(F_col),\n    V_return>=rho_col_return U_col_return>=rho_col_return V_col(F_col).\n\nAt epsilon=0 in the three-stage domain, V_three=U_col_three=V_col(F_col)=0:\nthe displayed weak comparison has equality and no payoff ratio is asserted.\nWhen epsilon>0 the comparison upper U_col is positive and the ratios\nV_three/U_col_three>=rho_col_three>1 and\nV_return/U_col_return>=rho_col_return>1 are defined and give strict\ndomination of every such collapsed completion. Epsilon>0 is automatic\nin the return parameter domain. Both factors diverge on their specified\ngrowing families. The comparison covers this exact retained prefix and\nsecond slope, with no claim about other partitions, slopes, or witnesses.\n\n(iv) For each of the two base growing words and each of its padded words,\nthe following bounds hold with each law evaluated on its corresponding\nword and ACTUAL physical horizon n; V denotes that law's attained payoff:\n\n    V_three>=(7/8)M^(-23/40),\n    V_return>=(3/2)M^(-7/12),\n    V>=(3/2)M^(-7/12)  for either law,\n    K_n(h)<=41M^(-5/6),\n    Q_n(h_three)<=64M^(-49/60),\n    Q_n(h_return)<=1600M^(-4/5),\n    L_lens_n(h_three)<=128Kc^18 M^(-49/60),\n    L_lens_n(h_return)<=3200Kc^24 M^(-4/5).\n\nWriting d_N=floor(log_2 N), the actual original benchmark obeys\n\n    B_n V >= [3lambda^3/(2*20^(7/12))]\n                       (lambda/2^(7/6))^(d_N)>1.\n\nConsequently each SPECIAL family supplies an attained static witness at\nEVERY integer horizon above its stated threshold with V>U_n^ZJ, and\nV/U_n^ZJ tends to infinity. Meanwhile\n\n    max{K_n(h),Q_n(h),L_lens_n(h)}=o(U_n^ZJ)\n\nalong each family, also with V_lens in place of L_lens_n. These results\nneither upper-bound unrestricted W_n on these words nor quantify over\narbitrary words. They give no comparison with all nonlinear scalar\nfunctions, all earlier adversaries, or the full complex legal region.\nThe original arbitrary-word asymptotic exact-profile theorem remains OPEN.\n\n\n# Joint proof of the stated multistage completion theorem\n\nAll construction parameters, vectors, lists, functions, channels and benchmark\nsequences have the definitions and ranges in the statement. For brevity,\nlocal M,A,B,C,a,p,q,r,Q,R,y,z,w denote the corresponding construction\nsymbols with subscript mc. A proximal pair means x=u+g with g=P_D_mc(x),\nwhere P_D_mc is Euclidean projection onto D_mc. Partial H denotes the\nconvex subdifferential of H. In the three-stage terminal calculation, local\nL_stage=B+a (written L in the displayed algebra) is a stage coefficient,\nnot the objective's smoothness constant, which is1 throughout the proof.\nThe notation E in that calculation means E_3. In the return calculation,\nd means d_R. The notation v_0 in each of these separate calculations is\nthe displacement from its indicated zero-height endpoint to x_n.\n\nThe only verified mathematical inputs are fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290\n(maximal unqueried-terminal prefix interpolation and signed residual\nnecessity), fact_id b98bb807d514e80d (the exact all-split benchmark,\nK_n, and B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3)), and fact_id dfde2fa326c908bf\n(the precise complex-polynomial transcript and universal lens, together\nwith the reciprocal-root Chebyshev identity and its ellipse estimate).\nTheir exact definitions are reproduced in the statement. In particular,\nthe benchmark here ranges over every split and the complex comparison is\nonly on Lens_n. No audit or unverified finding is a mathematical premise;\nall new geometry, terminal active-set algebra and reference comparisons\nare established jointly below.\n\n## 1. Static old function and every reached query\n\nLet D=conv{0,p,q,r} and\n\n    F_old(x)=max_(g in D){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.\n\nThe maximum is attained uniquely because D is compact and the objective\nis strictly concave; the maximizer is P_D(x). The projection normal\ncondition says, for u=x-g,\n\n    <u,b-g><=0 for every b in D.\n\nIt also says g maximizes the support J(u)=max(0,<p,u>,<q,u>,<r,u>).\nThus x=u+g, delta=J(u)=<g,u>, and\nF_old(x)=delta+||g||^2/2. Conversely, any displayed pair with g in D\nand this support condition is the UNIQUE projection pair. Tied support\nplanes do not make the envelope gradient ambiguous.\n\nFor any two old pairs, direct cancellation in the signed original\nresidual of fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290 gives\n\n    Q_xy=F_old(x)-F_old(y)+<g_x,y-x>+||g_x-g_y||^2/2\n        =<u_y,g_x-g_y><=0.\n\nThis works for every ordered pair, remote pairs, duplicate positions,\nand the zero optimum. To check global regularity directly, the two\nprojection normal inequalities with each other's projected vectors give\n\n    <x-x',P_D(x)-P_D(x')>>=||P_D(x)-P_D(x')||^2.\n\nCauchy--Schwarz makes the projection 1-Lipschitz. Evaluation of the\nattained maximum at its two maximizing vectors bounds\nF_old(x+d)-F_old(x)-<P_D(x),d> between0 and\n<P_D(x+d)-P_D(x),d>, hence between0 and ||d||^2. Thus F_old is\ndifferentiable with gradient P_D, which is 1-Lipschitz, and convex as\na supremum of affine functions. The slopes are independent: p has\nnonzero first coordinate, q has nonzero second coordinate, and r has\nnonzero third coordinate. Therefore D has interior, where P_D(x)=x\nand F_old has Hessian I. Its least global smoothness is exactly one.\n\nBecause 0 is a vertex of D, F_old>=0 and its complete minimum set is\n\n    {x:<p,x><=0, <q,x><=0, <r,x><=0}.\n\nIndeed this condition makes every objective term nonpositive. If any\nof the three inner products is positive, a sufficiently small positive\nmultiple of that vertex belongs to D and makes the objective positive.\nThe p inequality puts all minima in x_1<=0; origin is the unique\nclosest one to e_1 and the true distance is one.\n\nThe following table derives the actual static chronology. Its variable\nt=1+s, where s is the ordinary mass already spent in the current list;\ntherefore 1<=t<=M. The right endpoint t=M is also the selected following\nbridge query. Nonnegativity of every entry ensures every ordinary\npartial mass lies in this interval.\n\n| Stage | Proposed proximal point u | Current support height | Exhaustive other-plane comparisons |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| p | (1-t/M)e_1 | (1-t/M)/M | q height is minus twice this height; r height equals this height; zero is below it |\n| q | X-tq, X=-Ae_1 | (M-t)Q | p height=(-A+2t/M)/M<0; r height=-(M-t)Q/2<=0; zero is below q |\n| r | X_R-tr, X_R=y-BMq | (M-t)R | q height=-BMQ+tQ/2<0; p height=(2-A+2B-t/M)/M<0; zero is below r |\n| returned q | X_D-tq, X_D=z-CMr | (M-t)Q | r height=-CMR+tQ/2<0; p height=(-A+2t/M)/M<0; zero is below q |\n\nFor the first row, the p/r tie holds at EVERY p-stage query. Since p\nis an active support subgradient and x=u+p, projection uniqueness\nproves the actual gradient is p. It is not necessary to break that tie.\nAll strict comparisons in the other rows follow from B>=2 and\nA>=4B+4, including t=M. At zero height the zero plane may tie with\nother planes, but the displayed projection remains unique.\n\nThe endpoints and bridges follow by literal GD subtraction:\n\n    after L_0: x=p, u=0;\n    p-(AM+1)p = X=-Ae_1;\n    y=X-Mq=(2-A,-nu,0),    <q,y>=<r,y>=0;\n    (y+q)-(BM+1)q=X_R=y-BMq;\n    z=X_R-Mr=y-BMq-Mr;\n    <r,z>=0, <q,z>=M(1/2-B)Q, z_1=1-A+2B;\n    (z+r)-(CM+1)r=X_D=z-CMr;\n    w=X_D-Mq=z-CMr-Mq;\n    <q,w>=0, <r,w>=(MQ/2)(1-CB), w_1=2-A.\n\nFor h_three the last update is from z+r and reaches z-M epsilon r.\nFor h_return it is from w+q and reaches w-M epsilon q. Every bridge\nis an actual physical query and update. Lists were not collapsed into\noracle calls. A zero entry repeats the same jet and leaves the endpoint\nidentities unchanged.\n\nAt the old three-law terminal z-M epsilon r the first coordinate is\n1-A+2B-epsilon<0, the q support is\nMQ[(1+epsilon)/2-B]<0, and the r support is -M epsilon R<=0.\nAt the old return-law terminal w-M epsilon q the first coordinate is\n2-A+2epsilon<0, the q support is -M epsilon Q<0, and the r support\nis (MQ/2)(1-CB+epsilon)<0. Thus BOTH old terminal values are zero.\nOnly the unqueried terminal completion creates the stated new payoff.\n\n## 2. Complete conjugate-anchor lists and all prefix rows\n\nFor a prefix jet x=u+g, f=delta+||g||^2/2, its conjugate lower\nquadratic from fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290 simplifies, with no dropped constant, to\n\n    H_jet(v)=||v||^2/2+<u,v>-delta.\n\nWithin one constant-gradient stage both u and delta are affine in\nthe ordinary partial mass. Thus each intervening affine term is a\nconvex combination of the first-query and endpoint affine terms;\nit cannot enlarge their maximum. BOTH endpoint jets are physical\nprefix queries. This proves exact equality of the maximum over every\nphysical prefix jet with the endpoint maximum, rather than replacing\nthe interpolation problem by an endpoint relaxation.\n\nHere are all six/eight affine terms, including the original optimum:\n\n| Anchor u | Offset delta | Laws retaining it |\n|---|---|---|\n| 0 | 0 | both; p-stage bridge and optimum give the same affine term |\n| t_P=a e_1 | a/M | both; initial query |\n| y | 0 | both; q-stage ending bridge query |\n| t_Q=y+(M-1)q | (M-1)Q | both; FIRST q query |\n| z | 0 | both; r-stage ending bridge query |\n| t_R=z+(M-1)r | (M-1)R | both; FIRST r query |\n| w | 0 | return; returned-q ending query |\n| t_D=w+(M-1)q | (M-1)Q | return; FIRST returned-q query |\n\nThe affine anchors are distinct. The vectors q,r are independent and\northogonal to y, while y!=0. In the affine coordinate basis y,q,r,\ny,t_Q,z,t_R,w,t_D have respective coordinates\n(1,0,0),(1,M-1,0),(1,-BM,-M),(1,-BM,-1),\n(1,-(B+1)M,-(C+1)M),(1,-BM-1,-(C+1)M).\nThese are pairwise different for the stated parameters. Neither0 nor\nt_P=a e_1 equals any of them: representation of e_1 in the basis\ny,q,r has y coefficient -1/A, so t_P has negative y coefficient,\nwhereas the six displayed vectors have y coefficient1. The points0\nand t_P are distinct because a>0. Thus the distinct affine-anchor\ncounts are six and eight. In particular the\nold FIRST r query is retained after returning to q; its lower quadratic\ndoes not become redundant merely because the endpoint z is retained.\n\nDefine H_three as ||v||^2/2 plus the maximum of the first six affine\nterms, H_return using all eight, and F_three=H_three^*,\nF_return=H_return^*. All ordered old prefix inequalities and the\noptimum jet were proved in Section 1. The maximal-prefix construction\nof accepted fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290 therefore applies exactly, preserving EVERY old\nprefix position, gradient and value, including repeated queries. Its\nproof gives finite convex C1 globally 1-smooth conjugates. Fix these\nfunctions before running their words. Induction on the physical indices\nthen gives the same prefix updates; no online function change is used.\n\nThe new terminal gradients are not assumed equal to the old gradients.\nAt the terminal, x=v+conv(active anchor slopes) is the precise\nsubgradient optimality condition. It is checked separately below.\nSince each completed H is 1-strongly convex, that condition determines\nthe unique terminal gradient. Incoming AND outgoing terminal rows hold\nfor the resulting actual function: apply the interpolation necessity in\nfact_id 1252b8823bb5a290 to any two of its actual prefix, terminal and\noptimum samples. This gives Q_ij<=0 in both orientations for every\nordered pair. The formal Gram is a Gram of the actual vectors in R^3,\nhence is positive semidefinite. No queried-prefix comparison was deleted.\n\n## 3. Three-stage terminal, including every boundary\n\nLet L=B+a, E=t_Q-z=M L q+Mr, and\n\n    D_3=||E||^2=2A(L^2-L+B/2),\n    v_0=-M epsilon r,\n    b_3=<E,v_0>-(M-1)Q=Aa(epsilon-2/M),\n    theta_3=(b_3)_+/D_3,    v_3=v_0-theta_3 E.\n\nFor a in [1/2,1),\nL^2-L+B/2=B^2+B(2a-1/2)+a^2-a>=B^2+B/2-1/4>=B^2.\nThus D_3>=2AB^2 and 0<=theta_3<=epsilon/(2B^2)<=1/8.\nThis pays the convex-combination range and every denominator sign.\n\nAll six affine comparisons can be written explicitly:\n\n* z has value -theta_3<z,E>, with\n  <z,E>=2A(1/2-B)L<0. This value is nonnegative, and is\n  positive when theta_3>0.\n* t_Q minus z has affine value b_3-theta_3 D_3, which is zero\n  when b_3>=0 and nonpositive otherwise.\n* y and 0 have value zero because y is orthogonal to q and r.\n* t_R minus z has value (M-1)(<r,v_3>-R). Here\n  <r,v_3>=(MQ/2)(-B epsilon+a theta_3)<0 for epsilon>0.\n  At epsilon=0 this difference is instead -(M-1)R<0.\n* t_P minus z has value <t_P-z,v_3>-a/M. Directly,\n  <t_P-z,v_0>=-a epsilon and\n  <t_P-z,E>=2L[A(B-1/2)-a]+a>0, so this difference is negative.\n\nIf epsilon<2/M, theta_3=0 and z is active, along with y and 0;\nif epsilon=2/M, t_Q also ties but z alone may still be selected.\nIf epsilon>2/M, z and t_Q are active and theta_3 lies strictly\nbetween zero and one. In every case,\n\n    x_n=z+v_0=v_3+z+theta_3 E in partial H_three(v_3).\n\nAt epsilon=0 the terminal gradient and value are zero and z is a\nminimum. This covers M=2, epsilon=1 as well, where epsilon=2/M;\nthere is no hidden positive-theta assumption at that endpoint.\n\nThe active z term gives the exact conjugate value by completing a\nsquare:\n\n    V_three=2F_three(x_n)\n           =||v_0||^2-(b_3)_+^2/D_3\n           =AB epsilon^2-(Aa(epsilon-2/M)_+)^2/D_3.\n\nSince the loss is at most A epsilon^2/(2B^2),\n\n    AB epsilon^2(1-1/(2B^3))<=V_three<=AB epsilon^2.\n\nThe formula is continuous across the active-set boundary; it was\nderived from the full six-anchor maximum, not a terminal ansatz.\n\n## 4. Return terminal and the retained old first-r query\n\nThe feasible return parameter set itself implies M>=2B^2>=8 and\nepsilon>0. Epsilon=0 and small M outside this set are not conclusions\nof the return theorem. Set\n\n    d=C+a=2B+2-1/M,\n    E_R=t_R-w=Mq+Md r,\n    Dhat_R=1-d+(B/2)d^2,\n    D_R=||E_R||^2=2A Dhat_R,\n    v_0=-M epsilon q,\n    b_R=<E_R,v_0>-(M-1)R\n       =2A[(B-1/(2M))epsilon-Ba/(2M)],\n    theta_R=b_R/D_R,    v_R=v_0-theta_R E_R.\n\nThe derivative of 1-d+Bd^2/2 is Bd-1>0 in this range and\n2B+1<d<2B+2<=3B. The exact value at the lower comparison point is\n\n    1-(2B+1)+(B/2)(2B+1)^2\n        =2B^3+2B^2-3B/2.\n\nSince 2B^2-3B/2>0 for B>=2, the displayed exact lower-point\nidentity and monotonicity give Dhat_R>=2B^3.\n\nThe upper bound Dhat_R<=(9/2)B^3 follows by d<=3B and 1-d<0.\nWrite eta=(B-1/(2M))epsilon-Ba/(2M). Equivalently\neta=B epsilon-(epsilon+Ba)/(2M)<=B epsilon. For the lower\neta>=B epsilon/2 it suffices that\nB M epsilon>=epsilon+Ba. But B M epsilon>=2B^3>=B+1>=Ba+epsilon.\nHence\n\n    2B^3<=Dhat_R<=(9/2)B^3,\n    epsilon/(9B^2)<=theta_R<=epsilon/(2B^2)<=1/8,\n\nwith b_R>0. In particular the active combination is admissible,\nincluding the boundary M epsilon=2B^2 and epsilon=1 when feasible.\n\nThe complete eight-anchor screen is as follows.\n\n1. t_R and w tie because their affine difference is\n   b_R-theta_R D_R=0. The w value is positive:\n   <w,v_0>=0 and <w,E_R>=A d(1-CB)<0.\n2. For the old endpoint z, direct substitution, without omitting t_R,\n   gives\n\n       <r,v_R>/(Q/2)\n          =[M epsilon d(B-1/2)+(Ba/2)(Bd-1)]/Dhat_R.\n\n   Its first numerator term is at least 2B^2 d(B-1/2).\n   Since B-1/2>=3B/4 and d<=3B, this is at least B^2 d^2/2.\n   Also B Dhat_R<=B^2 d^2/2. The second numerator term is\n   strictly positive, so <r,v_R>>R throughout the feasible scope.\n   Using the t_R/w tie, the z-minus-w value equals\n   (M-1)(R-<r,v_R>)<0. Thus z is inactive even on the stated\n   return parameter boundary; this is not presumed from chronology.\n3. <q,v_R>=MQ[-epsilon+theta_R(B-1/(2M))]<0, because\n   theta_R B<=epsilon/(2B)<epsilon. Thus t_D is below w.\n   The t_Q affine value is (M-1)(<q,v_R>-Q)<0.\n4. y and 0 have value zero, below the positive w value.\n5. For E_P=t_P-w, the independent first-coordinate calculation is\n\n       <E_P,v_0>=2a epsilon,\n       <E_P,E_R>=a(d-2)+A d(CB-1)>=4AB^3.\n\n   Indeed d>=2B+1 and CB-1=2B^2+B-1>=2B^2. Consequently\n   <E_P,v_R><=2a epsilon-(4AB/9)epsilon<0, since\n   A>=4B+4 and B>=2 imply 4AB/9>2. Subtracting delta_P\n   makes the t_P-minus-w comparison still more negative.\n\nThese cover all eight terms with the original offsets. Since w and\nt_R are active and 0<theta_R<1,\n\n    x_n=w+v_0=v_R+w+theta_R E_R in partial H_return(v_R).\n\nThe exact value and loss follow from the active w term:\n\n    V_return=2F_return(x_n)=2A epsilon^2-b_R^2/D_R,\n    2A epsilon^2(1-1/(2B))<=V_return<=2A epsilon^2,\n    V_return>=3A epsilon^2/2.\n\nFor the lower value bound, b_R<=2AB epsilon and D_R>=4AB^3\ngive loss<=A epsilon^2/B. This completes the full feasible parameter\nscreen, including the epsilon and cap-equality boundaries.\n\n## 5. Complete new minimum sets, attainment and sharp normalization\n\nAll positive offsets in the anchor table are strictly positive, since\nM>=2, a>0, Q>0 and R>0. At v=0 exactly the zero-offset anchors\nare active. The subdifferential of a finite maximum of affine functions\nis the convex hull of its active slopes: every such convex combination\nis a supporting slope; a slope outside that hull is separated from it\nby a direction and cannot satisfy the supporting inequality along that\ndirection. Therefore\n\n    partial H_three(0)=conv{0,y,z},\n    partial H_return(0)=conv{0,y,z,w}.\n\nEach H(0)=0, so its conjugate F>=0. Optimality for the strictly\nconvex conjugate maximization says F(x)=0 precisely when its maximizer\nis v=0, equivalently x in partial H(0). These are the COMPLETE\nminimum sets, and the minimum value is attained and is zero.\n\nThe first coordinates of y,z,w are respectively 2-A, 1-A+2B,\n2-A, all strictly negative. Thus each completed minimum hull lies\nin x_1<=0 and contains origin. For any minimum x,\n||e_1-x||^2=1-2x_1+||x||^2>=1, with equality only at x=0.\nThe unique closest minimum is origin and the TRUE radius is exactly\none. Completion shrinks the old minimum cone to these bounded hulls;\nradius preservation has been proved from the new hulls, not assumed.\n\nFor 0<t<1/M sufficiently small, at v=t e_1 every nonzero\nzero-offset anchor has strictly negative affine value, and each\npositive-offset anchor has strictly negative affine value too. The\ninitial one requires t_P,1 t<delta_P, which is exactly t<1/M.\nThere is an open ball around such v on which the zero affine term\nalone wins, so H(v)=||v||^2/2 there. Optimality gives F(x)=||x||^2/2\non that same open ball, with Hessian I. Since global smoothness one\nwas already supplied by fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290, it is SHARP. There is no hidden\nL or distance renormalization. The exact terminal values in Sections\n3 and 4 are thus actual attained payoffs in the W_n=2R_n normalization\nin R^3.\n\n## 6. Exact growing parameters and physical counts\n\nFor each integer N>=2^60 choose\n\n    M=2N(N+1)+1,   A=M^(1/12),   B=M^(1/120),\n    epsilon=M^(-1/3),   T_Cheb=(2N+1)^2=2M-1.\n\nM>2^121 implies B>2. Since A=B^10>=2^9 B=512B>=4B+4 for\nB>=2, the first bridge hypothesis holds. Also M^(13/20)>2 in this\nrange, so M epsilon=M^(2/3)>=2M^(1/60)=2B^2. Thus these\nare inside the previously screened parameter sets, with epsilon>0.\nAccepted fact_id dfde2fa326c908bf gives each Chi_N mass 2N(N+1)/3. Three copies\nhave mass M-1 and length 3N. The initial list uses three increasing\ncopies, every later list three decreasing copies. The physical lengths,\nincluding every bridge, are therefore n_three=9N+3 and n_return=12N+4.\nThe guards in Section 1 cover every entry without executing the lists.\n\nThe exact value bounds give\n\n    V_three>=AB epsilon^2(1-1/(2B^3))>=(7/8)M^(-23/40),\n    V_return>=3A epsilon^2/2=(3/2)M^(-7/12).\n\nSince B(1-1/(2B^3))>=15/8>3/2, BOTH payoffs obey the common\nlower bound V>=(3/2)M^(-7/12). This uses the B factor in V_three,\nnot only a comparison of power exponents.\n\n## 7. Every original cut and the quadratic/lens channels\n\nWith P_j=sum_(i<j)h_i and T_j=sum_(i>j)h_i at the ORIGINAL\nphysical index, accepted fact_id b98bb807d514e80d defines\n\n    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2sum h),\n           max_j (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)]}.\n\nWrite increasing Chi_N entries as csc^2(k pi/(2N+1)),\nk=N,N-1,...,1. If theta=pi/(2N+1), concavity of sine gives\nsin((k-1)theta)>=(k-1)sin(k theta)/k. Thus consecutive increasing\nentry ratios are at most (k/(k-1))^2<=4. The first entry is <=4/3 for\nN>=2, because N pi/(2N+1)>=2pi/5>pi/3. Thus an increasing-copy\nentry h is <=9(1+preceding mass in that copy); reversing the copy\ngives h<=9(1+following mass in that copy), including the small last\nentry. Also sin(pi/(2N+1))>=2/(2N+1) gives max Chi_N<=T_Cheb/4<M.\nWithin-copy mass remains a lower bound for the corresponding full mass.\n\nThe following cut partition is complete. All denominators are at the\nphysical indices; no schedule compression or mass regrouping is used.\n\n| Physical cut | Bound on its K term | Reason |\n|---|---|---|\n| ordinary entry in L_0 | 41/(AM) | squared prefix ratio<=81; following mass includes AM+1 |\n| any later ordinary entry | 9/(A^2 M) | 1+P_j>=AM; h<=M and h<=9(1+T_j) give h^2/(1+2T_j)<=9M |\n| AM+1 | A^2/(2BM) | 1+P_j=M; following mass includes BM+1 |\n| BM+1 | B^2/(A^2 M) | 1+P_j>=AM; following mass>=M |\n| CM+1, return word | C^2/(A^2 M)<=(25/4)B^2/(A^2 M) | same mass guards; C<=5B/2 |\n| final 1+M epsilon | epsilon^2/A^2 | preceding mass>=AM; following mass is zero |\n| global Huber term | 1/(2AM) | total mass>=AM |\n\nThe last ordinary cut is licensed by DECREASING suffix order. An\nincreasing final list would not justify this table. After substituting\nthe parameter powers, the slow possible terms include\n\n    A^2/(2BM)=(1/2)M^(-101/120),\n    epsilon^2/A^2=M^(-5/6).\n\nAll other displayed terms decay faster than M^(-5/6), with constants\nat most 41. Since K is a maximum, BOTH complete words satisfy\nK_n(h)<=41M^(-5/6).\n\nEvery convex quadratic in any finite dimension diagonalizes, and its\nnormalized error is a convex combination of t product_j(1-h_j t)^2\nfor 0<=t<=1. Zero eigenvalues contribute no objective gap; choosing a\nfarther minimizer only increases the denominator. Consequently the\nentire quadratic channel is bounded by\nQ_n(h)=max_(0<=t<=1)t product_j(1-h_j t)^2.\nConversely, for each t in (0,1], the scalar quadratic f(x)=t x^2/2\nfrom x_0=1 attains that expression; a constant function gives zero\nat t=0. Continuity on [0,1] gives the maximum, so this expression is\nexactly the full convex-quadratic channel asserted in the statement.\n\nLet P_N denote the polynomial of one Chi_N copy from fact_id dfde2fa326c908bf. On\n[0,1], |P_N(t)|^2<=min(1,1/(T_Cheb t)). The second bound uses\n|Cheb_(2N+1)(sqrt(t))|<=1. For the first, set sqrt(t)=sin(phi):\nthe odd Chebyshev identity reduces to\nsin((2N+1)phi)/((2N+1)sin(phi)). The finite unit-complex-number\nidentity sin(k phi)/sin(phi)=sum_(j=0)^(k-1)\nexp(i(k-1-2j)phi) for positive integer k gives\n|sin(k phi)|<=k|sin(phi)|; the zero-sine case follows by continuity.\nAt t=0 the polynomial value is one, which is the meaning of its\ndisplayed min bound at zero.\n\nThere are nine Chebyshev copies for h_three, twelve for h_return.\nWith u=T_Cheb t, the actual bridge coefficients divided by T_Cheb\nare <=A,B,1 (three), and <=A,B,C,1 (return). For each\nc in {A,B,C}, cM+1<=c(2M-1) follows from c(M-1)>=1, and\n1+M epsilon<=2M-1 because M>=2, epsilon<=1. The complete\nquadratic expressions are therefore bounded by\n\n    T_Cheb^(-1)u(1+Au)^2(1+Bu)^2(1+u)^2 min(1,u^(-9)),\n    T_Cheb^(-1)u(1+Au)^2(1+Bu)^2(1+Cu)^2(1+u)^2\n                         min(1,u^(-12)).\n\nFor u<=1 use 1+c u<=2c; for u>=1 use 1+c u<=2c u.\nThe latter products retain u^(-2) and u^(-3), respectively. The\nobjective at u=0 is zero. T_Cheb>=M and C<=5B/2 give\n\n    Q_n(h_three)<=64 A^2 B^2/T_Cheb<=64M^(-49/60),\n    Q_n(h_return)<=256 A^2 B^2 C^2/T_Cheb<=1600M^(-4/5).\n\nThis bounds all convex quadratic witnesses, not general scalar nonlinear\nor higher-dimensional smooth convex functions.\n\nFor the complex comparison the EXACT named universal lens is\n\n    Lens_n={zeta=alpha+i beta:\n            alpha-alpha^2-beta^2>=|beta|cot(pi/(n+2))}.\n\nAccepted fact_id dfde2fa326c908bf puts it inside the full cycle-legal region. If\nn>=N then Lens_n is also inside Lens_N, because cot(pi/(n+2))\nincreases with n. The accepted ellipse proof for an N-copy gives\n|zeta||P_N(zeta)|^2<=cosh^2(2)/T_Cheb on Lens_N. When\nT_Cheb|zeta|<=1, the direct product gives instead\n\n    |P_N(zeta)|<=exp(sum Chi_N |zeta|)<=exp(1/6)<=cosh(2),\n\nsince sum Chi_N=(T_Cheb-1)/6. The last constant comparison follows\nfrom exp(1/6)<=sum_(k>=0)(1/6)^k=6/5 and cosh(2)>=1+2=3.\nCombining the two regimes, with\nKc=cosh(2), yields\n\n    |P_N(zeta)|^2<=Kc^2 min(1,1/(T_Cheb|zeta|)).\n\nAt zeta=0 the polynomial is one and Gap_h(0)=0 because every edge\nweight is zero; the min bound is interpreted by that value. At every\nnonzero point of the lens the accepted maximal complex transcript obeys\nGap_h(zeta)<=(2alpha-|zeta|^2)|Poly_n(zeta)|^2<=2|zeta||Poly_n(zeta)|^2.\nWith u=T_Cheb|zeta|>0, the complete transcript estimates are\n\n    Gap_h_three(zeta)<=2Kc^18 T_Cheb^(-1)u\n         (1+Au)^2(1+Bu)^2(1+u)^2 min(1,u^(-9)),\n    Gap_h_return(zeta)<=2Kc^24 T_Cheb^(-1)u\n         (1+Au)^2(1+Bu)^2(1+Cu)^2(1+u)^2 min(1,u^(-12)).\n\nFor 0<u<=1, bound 1+c u by2c for each c in {A,B,C,1}.\nFor u>=1, bound it by2c u; the resulting powers are u^(-2)\nand u^(-3), respectively, and are at most1. Therefore\n\n    sup_(Lens_n) Gap_h_three <=128Kc^18M^(-49/60),\n    sup_(Lens_n) Gap_h_return<=3200Kc^24M^(-4/5).\n\nIf the channel is augmented by the canonical Huber maximum V_lens,\nits extra term is covered in the cut table. These bounds assert NOTHING\nabout sup_(D_h) Gap_h over the larger schedule-dependent complex\nregion, or about all scalar functions.\n\n## 8. Original all-split benchmark and actual padded horizons\n\nHere C_n,B_n are benchmark sequences, distinct from construction scalars\nC,B. Use EXACTLY the accepted all-split recurrences\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nAccepted fact_id b98bb807d514e80d gives B_n<=1+4n^rho_crude, rho_crude=log_2(3).
