Fixed cosh(2) constants do not affect the limit.\nThus all three named channels are o(U_n^ZJ) on each selected family\nat its actual horizon.\n\nTo prove V>U a LOWER bound on B_n is necessary. The all-split\nrecurrences directly give C_n>=Omega(C_(n-1),1)>C_(n-1), and\nbalanced splits give C_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j for lambda=1+sqrt(2).\nSelecting the split j=n-1 in B_n gives\nB_n>=G(C_(n-1),1)>=2C_(n-1)-1. For d_N=floor(log_2 N),\nboth physical families obey n-1>=2^(d_N+3)-1, and consequently\nB_n>=lambda^(d_N+3). Meanwhile M<=5N^2<20*4^(d_N). Hence\nthe common attained payoff satisfies\n\n    B_n V >= [3lambda^3/(2*20^(7/12))]\n                       (lambda/2^(7/6))^(d_N) >1.\n\nThis is an exact analytic estimate. Explicitly, lambda>12/5,\n2^(7/6)<9/4 (equivalently 2^19<3^12), and 20^(7/12)<8\n(use 5^7<2^21). Thus the prefactor is >324/125>2 and the\nbase is >16/15>1. The payoff exceeds U at EVERY claimed\nN>=2^60 and the ratio diverges. No fitted profile constant, assumed\nbenchmark asymptotic, balanced-only defining recurrence, or DP was used.\n\nFor an arbitrary sufficiently large integer horizon n, use\n\n    N=floor((n-3)/9),  k=n-(9N+3) in {0,...,8},\n    or N=floor((n-4)/12), k=n-(12N+4) in {0,...,11}.\n\nInsert k zeros at the VERY BEGINNING of the appropriate known word.\nThey repeat the already retained initial jet, so the exact full-prefix\nmaximum H is unchanged. The same static F has the same terminal value,\nsharp smoothness and closest radius. The terminal remains unqueried.\n\nFor K, every new zero cut has numerator zero and every old cut's\npreceding/following MASS is unchanged. Quadratic products acquire only\nfactors one. Lens_n shrinks. In the benchmark estimates use this\nactual padded n, which satisfies n<=9N+11<=16N for the three-stage\nlaw and n<=12N+15<=16N for the return law at these large N,\nand has n-1 at least the unpadded value. Explicit sufficient start\nhorizons are 9*2^60+3 and 12*2^60+4. Thus the argument supplies\none selected attained word for each large n; it does not quantify over\nevery length-n word.\n\n## 9. Precisely defined collapsed policy and its zero-tail boundary\n\nThis comparison retains the construction's p=e_1/M, initial point e_1,\nfirst ordinary list and selected bridge AM+1. It treats ALL remaining\nsteps before the final bridge as one ordinary block, whose total is\nM_2-1. Directly counting the original masses gives\n\n    M_2=(B+2)M          for h_three,\n    M_2=(B+C+3)M=(3B+4)M  for h_return.\n\nIts specified static second slope is\n\n    q_2=(-2e_1+sqrt(2A-4)e_2)/M_2,\n    ||q_2||^2=2A/M_2^2.\n\nFor the static envelope over conv{0,p,q_2}, a first-stage proximal\npoint u=(1-t/M)e_1, 1<=t<=M, has p support (1-t/M)/M>=0,\nq_2 support -2(1-t/M)/M_2<=0, and zero support0. Thus p is active\nand x=u+p has projection p onto that triangle. The projection\nregularity argument of Section 1 applies to this compact convex triangle\nas well, proving that F_col_old is a legal member of the collapsed class.\nAfter the first bridge, X=-Ae_1. At ordinary partial\nmass s with t=1+s in [1,M_2],\n\n    <q_2,X-tq_2>=(M_2-t)||q_2||^2>=0,\n    <p,X-tq_2>=(-A+2t/M_2)/M<0.\n\nThus every physical query of THIS collapsed policy is legal, including\nthe old BM+1 and CM+1 steps now acting as individual ordinary entries.\nLet y_2=X-M_2 q_2. The last queried jet is x=y_2+q_2,\ng=q_2, f=||q_2||^2/2. For EVERY legal smooth-convex completion\nof THIS entire prefix with its retained zero optimum, the descent\ninequality at that jet gives\n\n    f(y_2)<=f(y_2+q_2)-||q_2||^2/2=0.\n\nSince the retained optimum has value zero, y_2 is forced to be a\nminimum. The final physical step 1+M epsilon reaches\ny_2-M epsilon q_2, and smoothness about this forced minimum gives\n\n    2f(x_n)<=2A(M epsilon/M_2)^2.\n\nThe true radius for every such completion is also one, rather than\nan assumed inherited denominator. The retained initial jet is\ng_0=p and f_0=1/M-1/(2M^2). For any new minimizer x_star of value\nzero, the original interpolation necessity from fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290 gives\n\n    Q_(0,star)=f_0+<p,x_star-e_1>+||p||^2/2\n             =x_star,1/M<=0.\n\nSo all new minima have first coordinate <=0, and the retained origin\nis the unique closest one to e_1. This proves the bound for every\ncompletion of that precise prefix with TRUE radius one.\n\nWhen epsilon>0, the collapsed upper is positive and division is\nlegitimate. The three-stage witness-to-upper ratio is at least\n\n    [B(B+2)^2/2](1-1/(2B^3)),\n\nand the return witness-to-upper ratio is at least\n\n    (3/4)(3B+4)^2.\n\nBoth factors exceed one and diverge on the chosen growing words,\nwhere epsilon=M^(-1/3)>0. In the return parameter range epsilon>0\nis automatic. Thus the substantive advertised growing-family comparison\nholds, with exactly the stated collapsed policy as the comparison class.\n\nAt epsilon=0 in the three-stage parameter range, its formula in Section 3\ngives V_three=0, and U_col_three=0. Every collapsed completion has nonnegative\nterminal value and the upper bound zero, hence V_col(F_col)=0. Thus the weak\nmultiplicative inequality has equality at this boundary; its ratio is not\nasserted. For epsilon>0, division by the explicitly positive upper comparison\nU_col gives the two stated factors. For B>=2,\nrho_col_three>=15>1 and rho_col_return>=75>1, so the new payoff is\nstrictly greater than every payoff in the respective collapsed class. A\nparticular collapsed completion may have payoff zero; no division by that\npayoff is needed. In the return range epsilon>0 is forced by M epsilon>=2B^2.\nOn the specified growing words B=M^(1/120) tends to infinity, so both factors\ntend to infinity. This comparison is confined to the exactly specified\nprefix, second slope and retained optimum.\n\n## 10. Original normalization and scope\n\nFor either attained function F, its minimum is0, the unique closest\nminimizer to e_1 is0, the squared radius is1, and its least global gradient\nLipschitz constant is1. Thus V=2F(x_n) is exactly the project's W_n=2R_n\nnormalized payoff. Replacing F by L F for any L>0 makes the prescribed\nupdates x_next=x-(h_i/L)grad(L F)(x)=x-h_i grad F(x), with the same points\nand the same payoff. Therefore W_n(h)>=V for the complete function class\nin every stated case.\n\nFor each of the two specific padded word families, Sections 6--8 prove\nV>U_n^ZJ at every integer horizon above its stated threshold, V/U_n^ZJ\ntends to infinity, and the three exactly named reference channels are\no(U_n^ZJ), including the canonical Huber augmentation of the universal\nlens. All padding counts and benchmark subscripts use actual physical\nindices. These are selected-word lower witnesses and comparisons with\nspecified reference classes only. There is no bound on unrestricted W_n\nfrom above, no extension to all nonlinear scalar witnesses or the full\ncomplex legal region, and no arbitrary-word partition theorem. No\nterminal claim about its unscreened off-line continuation is made.\n\nThe decisive full-suffix diagnostic is policy2: a maximal completion\nfixed BEFORE GD preserves every entry jet and realizes every remaining\noriginal step. Its exact positive terminal payoff is asymptotically\nnegligible relative to the ORIGINAL benchmark. This is a limitation of\nthis specific continuation, not an upper bound on unrestricted W_n.\n\n## 1. Recall, accepted inputs, and the original benchmark\n\nRead in full: prior report20618061fa719178, its fixed-plane failure\n62e302b04457911c, and the small-ratio example3f56a6deb1e5bd53.\nThe relevant earlier audit0ebf0c159cc211e6 was recalled as awareness.\nThe dispatch guidance is90afbd2617b3aa0a. Its specialized entry is re-proved below; no unverified\nLaw1 or Law2 is treated as an accepted building block.\n\nAccepted correctness inputs:\n\n* fact_id1252b8823bb5a290, statement and constructive maximal-prefix\n  proof. For all original prefix jets and the zero optimum, define\n  y_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2. The conjugate of\n  H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_i(<y_i,v>-delta_i) is a fixed convex C1 globally\n  1-smooth interpolant preserving every such jet, and is the pointwise\n  maximal completion of THAT prefix. No prior anchor may be deleted.\n  The original signed ordered residual is\n  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.\n* fact_idb98bb807d514e80d, full statement/proof. For every full physical\n  word h of length n, K_n(h) is the maximum of the global Huber\n  1/(1+2 sum h) and EVERY single-cut term\n  (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)], with P_j=sum_(t<j)h_t and\n  T_j=sum_(t>j)h_t. These are attained lower witnesses. The ORIGINAL\n  all-split benchmark below has B_n<=1+4n^p, p=log_2(3).\n* fact_iddfde2fa326c908bf, full relevant polynomial statement and\n  constructive interpolation proof. The full polynomial is\n  Poly_t(z)=product_(0<=i<t)(1-h_i z). In particular the real scalar\n  quadratic with curvature1 has full-word payoff |Poly_n(1)|^2.\n  No universal-complex-lens restriction is substituted for the full\n  smooth-convex class in the argument below.\n\nFor x,y>=0 define\n\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.\n\nThe exact original recurrences are C_0=B_0=1 and\n\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),\n    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nEvery original integer split is retained. W_n(h) is the supremum of\n2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite dimensions, all\nconvex differentiable functions with L-Lipschitz gradient and a chosen\nminimizer x_star, using x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). The examples\nhere have the TRUE closest initial radius1, and hence their normalized\nvalues are actual lower witnesses for W_n(h). They give no W_n upper.\n\nAll norms/inner products are Euclidean. Local symbols have the suffix\n_fc in the attached glossary; unsuffixed forms below are local aliases.\nIn particular R below is ||r||^2, distinct from the original objective\nR_n=W_n/2; its shared name is Rnorm_fc, and local Q is Qnorm_fc.\nH_m is H_fc,m, local Y,Z,W of the return diagnostic are Y_ret_fc,\nZ_ret_fc,W_ret_fc, and every other introduced symbol is explicitly\ndefined in its section and glossary.json. All max-affine weights are1.\nEvery listed offset is explicit.\n\n## 2. The unchanged FULL word and a paid four-query entry\n\nFix ANY integer m>=7, tau=1/4, and set\n\n    u=1/(4m), lambda=1+sqrt2,\n    beta=1-u, c=lambda*(1+u), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a*c^l,H_l), 0<=l<m,\n    b_l=1+a*c^l, h=H_m, n=2^m-1.\n\nEvery child uses this SAME enclosing m and tau. No reset, padding,\ntruncation or permutation occurs. The first four original steps are\nexactly (b_0,b_1,b_0,b_2); all n-4 remaining entries are kept.\n\nWrite S_l=sum H_l and v_h=a/(c-2). Direct induction on\n1+S_(l+1)=2(1+S_l)+a*c^l gives\n\n    1+S_l=v_h*c^l+(1-v_h)*2^l.\n\nHere c-2>1/lambda>a, so 0<v_h<1, and 2<c<3.\nAlso beta>=27/28, a<1/2. Every first-occurrence bridge ratio\na*c^l/(1+S_l) is smaller than c-2<1. These are the original masses.\n\nDefine the literal entry data in R^3, with its standard basis e1,e2,e3:\n\n    M=2+a, s=M-1=1+a, D=beta, E=beta*c,\n    Delta=E-M,\n    d=a/[2(1+beta)], p=e1,\n    q=-d*e1+sqrt(a*d-d^2)*e2, Q=||q||^2=a*d,\n    k=beta/(2M), r=-k*q+k*sqrt(Q)*e3, R=||r||^2=2k^2 Q,\n    y=-a*e1-q,\n    z=y-beta*q-M*r=y-(beta/2)*(q+sqrt(Q)*e3),\n    tR=z+s*r.\n\nAll quantities are fixed from the known full word before GD. The entry\nhas M0=M1=1, first ratio a<1/2 and second bridge excess beta. Its third\nordinary block is the actual single step b_0, whose mass is M-1. Thus\nthis is the earlier Law1's j=0 entry, but its proof is fully repeated.\n\nThe apparent boundary cancellation is paid exactly:\n\n    Delta=2u/[lambda(1+u)]-lambda*u^2>0,\n    0<Delta<1/14,       0<k<1/4.\n\nIndeed positivity is equivalent to lambda^2*u*(1+u)<2, which follows\nfrom lambda^2<6 and u<=1/28. The upper bound follows by dropping the\nnegative term and using lambda>1 and u<=1/28. In particular E>M.\n\nThe identities needed for EVERY anchor are\n\n    y.q=y.r=z.r=0,      z.q=-beta*Q/2,\n    q.r=-kQ,           tR.r=sR,\n    y_1=-a+d<0,\n    z_1=-a+(1+beta/2)d<0,\n    (tR)_1=-a+(1+beta-k)d<-a/2.\n\nSet Dict=conv{0,p,q,r} and\n\n    J(w)=max(0,p.w,q.w,r.w),\n    F0(x)=max_(g in Dict){x.g-||g||^2/2}.\n\nThe four ORIGINAL queried jets from x_0=e1 are exactly\n\n| query t | x_t | g_t | proximal point x_t-g_t | f_t |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 0 | e1 | p | 0 | 1/2 |\n| 1 | -a e1=y+q | q | y | Q/2 |\n| 2 | y-beta q=tR+r | r | tR | sR+R/2 |\n| 3 | z+r | r | z | R/2 |\n\nAt proximal0 every homogeneous plane has height0, and p is the unique\nprojection maximizer since x_0=p lies in Dict. At y, the q,r,zero\nheights are0 and the p-height is negative. At tR the r-height is sR>0,\nthe q-height is (-beta+k)Q<0, and the p-height is negative by the last\ndisplay. At z the r and zero heights are0, the q-height is negative,\nand the p-height is negative. These exhaustive guards select the stated\ngradients; a support-plane tie does not make the envelope gradient\nambiguous. The updates are precisely b_0,b_1,b_0,b_2 and reach\n\n    x_1=-a e1,\n    x_2=y-beta q,\n    x_3=x_2-(M-1)r=z+r,\n    x_4=x_3-(1+E)r=z-E r.\n\nFor F0 the gradients are Euclidean projections onto Dict. If g is\nthis projection and w=x-g, its normal condition says\nw.b<=w.g for EVERY b in Dict, not merely neighboring slopes. Then\nF0(x)=J(w)+||g||^2/2. For any two actual samples, including optimum\nsamples and all future repetitions, direct expansion gives the COMPLETE\nordered identity\n\n    Q_ij=<w_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                         (A)\n\nThe inequality is exactly the normal condition at j with b=g_i.\nThus every remote and optimum row is covered. Projection optimality at\ntwo points gives <x-x',g-g'> >=||g-g'||^2; this proves global1-smoothness\nand the attained maximum gives convexity and C1 differentiability.\nDict has interior because p,q,r are independent, and on that interior\nthe projection is the identity. Smoothness1 is sharp.\n\nThis entry proof uses no earlier unverified claim. In Law1 terminology\nM1=1 so its old tQ endpoint duplicates y and its terminal correction\nweight is0; there is no unrestricted use of the earlier positive-weight\nformula or omission of its theta<=1 requirement.\n\n## 3. Policy1: changing gradient on the OLD edge, then full absorption\n\nKeep the single F0 above for EVERY query of the full H_m. Its offsets\nare all0, its weights all1, and x_0=e1. No completion is made.\n\nAt the FIRST new query x_4=z-E r define\n\n    theta_edge=k*Delta,       0<theta_edge<1/56.\n\nThe actual gradient is the varying edge point g_4=theta_edge*q in\nconv{0,q}, not the old full q slope. This is proved by its actual\nproximal point w_4=x_4-theta_edge*q:\n\n    q.w_4=0,\n    p.w_4=-a+d<0,\n    r.w_4=kQ*(theta_edge-2E k)<0.\n\nThe last sign uses Delta<E. Zero and q are tied at height0, with every\nother plane strictly below. The edge weight lies in[0,1], so this is\nthe actual unique projection and\n\n    F0(x_4)=theta_edge^2 Q/2.\n\nThe NEXT original step is h_4=b_0=1+a. It gives\n\n    x_5=x_4-(1+a)*theta_edge*q.\n\nAll three polar-cone inequalities are now strict:\n\n    q.x_5=-a*theta_edge*Q<0,\n    r.x_5=k^2 Q[-2E+(1+a)Delta]<0,\n    p.x_5=-a+d+a*theta_edge*d<0.\n\nFor the second sign, 1+a<2 and Delta<E. For the third, a*theta_edge<1\ngives -a+d+a*theta_edge*d<-a+2d=-a*beta/(1+beta)<0.\n\nThe COMPLETE minimum set is\n\n    C0={x:p.x<=0, q.x<=0, r.x<=0},    min F0=0.\n\nThe inequalities suffice directly in the maximum defining F0. If any\none is violated, a small positive multiple of its slope belongs to Dict\nand gives a positive value, proving necessity. Since p=e1, every\nminimizer has first coordinate<=0. Policy2: maximal completion fixed BEFORE the full run\n\nThe original four queried jets of Section2, plus the zero optimum,\ngive precisely the affine anchor list\n\n    (0,0), (y,0), (z,0), (tR,sR).\n\nThe initial query contributes another copy of(0,0); keep that physical\nquery although its identical affine term need only be printed once.\nThere is no omitted q-start anchor: its ordinary list is EMPTY, so the\nfirst q anchor is exactly y. The FIRST r query contributes tR with\npositive height sR and is retained. The following r query contributes z.\n\nFreeze BEFORE GD the single function\n\n    Hmax(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,y.v,z.v,tR.v-sR),\n    Fmax(x)=sup_v{x.v-Hmax(v)}. Directly,\nat g=p only the0 anchor is needed, at g=q the y anchor is active,\nand at g=r all0,y,z,tR terms are0. Fmax need not preserve the future jets that F0 would\nhave produced after time4; they are DIFFERENT policies. In the actual\nFmax run EVERY preterminal jet, including all later ones proved below,\nis a jet of this ONE frozen function. No later completion is applied.\n\n### 4.1 Global function, minimum, radius and sharp smoothness\n\nHmax is finite continuous1-strongly convex with quadratic growth. For\neach x its conjugate maximizer g(x) exists uniquely and satisfies\nx in partial Hmax(g(x)). Strong subgradient inequalities at x,x'\ngive <x-x',g(x)-g(x')> >=||g(x)-g(x')||^2. Maximizing inequalities\ngive differentiability with grad Fmax=g, so Fmax is convex C1 globally\n1-smooth. This is also the construction proof of accepted1252.\n\nAt v=0 the active affine slopes are precisely0,y,z; the tR term has\nstrictly negative offset-sR. Consequently\n\n    argmin Fmax=conv{0,y,z},         min Fmax=0.       (C)\n\nTo justify completeness, Fmax is differentiable, so x minimizes it\niff grad Fmax(x)=0 iff x belongs to partial Hmax(0). Hmax(0)=0 then\ngives the minimum value0. Both nonzero vertices in(C) have negative\nfirst coordinate; hence origin is the unique closest minimizer to e1\nand the TRUE radius is exactly1. No smaller denominator is assumed.\n\nFor small v=t e1, t>0, the y,z terms are strictly negative and the\ntR term remains strictly negative. A nonempty open neighborhood has\nHmax(v)=||v||^2/2. Its conjugate therefore has an open Hessian-I\nregion. The global smoothness bound1 is SHARP.\n\n### 4.2 One exact region containing EVERY physical suffix query\n\nFor EVERY real rho<=1 the actual point/gradient/value are\n\n    x=z+rho*r,      grad Fmax(x)=rho*r,\n    Fmax(x)=rho^2 R/2.                               (D)\n\nIndeed y.r=z.r=0 and tR.r=sR, so at v=rho*r the four affine values\nare0,0,0,sR(rho-1). They are ALL checked, including the first r\nanchor. The z term is active and x=v+z belongs to partial Hmax(v).\nUniqueness supplies the actual gradient and conjugate evaluation gives\n(D). There is NO lower bound on rho: negative amplitudes are allowed.\nThe positive-amplitude restriction rho<=1 is crucial and is proved\nfor EVERY forthcoming original index; it is never silently removed.\n\nAt time3, x_3=z+r and rho_3=1. The actual step at index t has level\n\n    ell(t)=nu_2(t+1),     h_t=1+a*c^ell(t),\n\nwhere nu_2(j) is the largest nonnegative integer l such that 2^l divides\nthe positive integer j. This exact index formula follows by induction\non H_(l+1)=(H_l,b_l,H_l): its central index has t+1=2^l, and in the\nright child nu_2(2^l+j)=nu_2(j) for 0<j<2^l.\n\nLet s_2(t) count the ones in the binary expansion of the nonnegative\ninteger t. It proves the complete run by induction. At t=3 it is1.\nThe identity s_2(t+1)-s_2(t)=1-nu_2(t+1) gives\n\n    rho_(t+1)/rho_t=-beta*c^(nu_2(t+1)-1)=1-h_t.\n\nIf t is even, rho_t<0. If t is odd and t>=3, its binary expansion has\nat least two ones, so s_2(t)>=2 and\n0<rho_t<=beta^(t-3)<=1, with strict inequality for t>3. Thus EVERY\nquery lies in(D).
